{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:base_dual_1}$}}
commutes by
[
Lip09
, Lemma 4.6.5]
,
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:base_dual_2}$}}
commutes by Lemma
5.1
, and other squares commute by naturality.
∎

6.
On the pullbacks in the regular case

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of schemes. Suppose that
L
is a line bundle on
Y
. One can always simply define a pullback morphism on the vector bundle
\mathrm{GW}
-theory as

f^{*}:\mathrm{GW}^{[i]}(Y,L)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[i]}(X,f^{*}L)

by the dg form functor
(f^{*},\beta):(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(Y)),\sharp_{L})\to(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X)),\sharp_{f^{*}L})
where
\beta:f^{*}[A,L]\to[f^{*}A,f^{*}L]
is the canonical morphism, cf.
[
Sch17
, Section 9.4]
. In Section
4
, we have constructed the pullbacks in the case of flat morphisms. Unfortunately, the general pullback can not be simply defined in the coherent Hermitian
K
-theory. The situation is already reflected in the situation of coherent
K
-theory. If
f
is of finite tor-dimension cf.
[
Har66
, II.4, p.99]
, then Serre’s formula comes into the picture

f^{*}:G_{0}(Y)\to G_{0}(X),A\mapsto\sum_{i\geq 0}(-1)^{i}\mathrm{Tor}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}^{i}(A,\mathcal{O}_{X}).

If we assume that
Y
is a regular scheme, then the canonical morphism
K_{0}(Y)\to G_{0}(Y)
is an isomorphism. Via this identification, the pullbacks on
K
-theory coincide with that on
G
-theory.

6.1.
Pullbacks in the regular case

Suppose that
X
and
Y
are regular schemes throughout this subsection. Our aim in this section is to study the general pullbacks of
\mathrm{GW}
-theory in the regular case.

Theorem 6.2
.

Suppose that
f:X\to Y
is a morphism of regular schemes. Assume that
L
is a line bundle on
Y
, and that
\rho:L\to EL
is the associated residue complex of
L
. Then, the exact dg functor

f^{\ast}:\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(Y))\to\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X))

induces a map of Grothendieck-Witt spectra

f^{\ast}:\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(Y,EL\right)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(X,Ef^{\ast}L\right)

which respects composition, i.e. if
f
and
g
are two consecutive morphisms, then
f^{\ast}g^{\ast}=(gf)^{\ast}
.

Proof.

Form the following dg functors

\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Y))\leftarrow\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(Y))\xrightarrow{f^{*}}\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X))\to\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))

which induce the following dg form functors

(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Y)),\sharp_{EL})\xleftarrow{(\mathrm{id},\rho)}(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(Y)),\sharp_{L})\xrightarrow{(f^{*},\beta)}(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X)),\sharp_{f^{*}L})\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id},\rho)}(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X)),\sharp_{Ef^{*}L}).

The required morphism of coherent Grothendieck-Witt spectra

f^{*}:\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,EL)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,Ef^{*}L)

is defined to be the composition

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,EL)\xleftarrow[\simeq]{\rho}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,L)\xrightarrow{f^{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,f^{*}L)\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\rho}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,Ef^{*}L).

The final claim that pullback functor
f^{*}
respects composition follows from the fact that the vector bundle
\mathrm{GW}
-theory respects the composition, cf.
[
Sch17
, Section 9.3]
or Lemma
4.18
.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a flat morphism, and
L
be a line bundle on
X
. Then, the isomorphism
Ef^{\ast}(\rho):Ef^{\ast}L\to Ef^{\ast}EL
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(X,Ef^{\ast}L\right)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(X,Ef^{\ast}EL\right)

of Grothendieck-Witt spectra. Under this equivalence, two pullbacks defined in Theorem
4.11
and Theorem
6.2
coincide.

Proof.

By naturality, there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{f^{\ast}L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}(\rho)}
\textstyle{Ef^{\ast}L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Ef^{\ast}(\rho)}
\textstyle{f^{\ast}EL\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{Ef^{\ast}EL.}

Let
\tilde{\rho}
be the composition
f^{\ast}L\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}\rho}f^{\ast}EL\to Ef^{\ast}EL
.
We shall show that the following diagram

(14)

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(X,f^{\ast}L\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\rho}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(Y,L\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(X,Ef^{\ast}EL\right)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(Y,EL\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}

commutes.
The result follows from the fact that the following square

\textstyle{[f^{\ast}A,f^{\ast}L]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}(\rho)}
\textstyle{f^{\ast}[A,L]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{[f^{\ast}A,f^{\ast}EL]}
\textstyle{f^{\ast}[A,EL]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}

commutes for any
A\in\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(Y))
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

The diagram (
14
) still commutes if we assume more generally that
X
is a Gorenstein scheme with an ample family of line bundles. However,
\rho
and
\tilde{\rho}
may not be equivalences in this generality.

6.5.
Projection formula

Let
\iota:Z\to X
be a morphism, and let
\mathcal{G}
be a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. There is a canonical morphism

(15)

\wp:\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G}\to\iota_{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\iota^{*}\mathcal{G})

given by the composition

\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G}\to\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\mathcal{G}\to\iota_{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\iota^{*}\mathcal{G}),

cf.
[
Lip09
, Example 3.4.6, p.107]
. This morphism is an isomorphism if
\mathcal{G}
is locally free.

Let
I
be a residue complex and
L
be a line bundle on
X
. By the local nature of the definition of
\iota^{\Delta}
, there is a natural isomorphism of complexes (cf.
[
Con00
, (3.3.9)]
):

\theta:\iota^{\Delta}I\otimes\iota^{\ast}L\to\iota^{\Delta}(I\otimes L).

Assume that
\iota
is finite, by
[
Har66
, Lemma VI.4.1, p.335]
, the natural morphism

\textnormal{Hom}_{\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))}(\iota_{\ast}(\iota^{\Delta}I\otimes\iota^{\ast}L),I\otimes L)\xrightarrow{\cong}\textnormal{Hom}_{D^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))}(\iota_{\ast}(\iota^{\Delta}I\otimes\iota^{\ast}L),I\otimes L).

is an isomorphism and then by
[
Har66
, Proposition III.6.9(c), p.174]
(and
[
Con00
, (2.2.10)]
for clarification), the diagram

\textstyle{\iota_{\ast}(\iota^{\Delta}I\otimes\iota^{\ast}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wp^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 16$}}}
\textstyle{\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\Delta}I\otimes L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Tr}}
\textstyle{\iota_{\ast}\iota^{\Delta}(I\otimes L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Tr}}
\textstyle{I\otimes L.}

is commutative.

Proposition 6.6

(Projection formula)
.

Let
\iota:Z\to X
be a finite morphism of regular schemes. Let
I
be a residue complex on
X
and let
L
be a line bundle on
X
.
Then the following diagram of Grothendieck-Witt spectra

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}I)\times\mathrm{GW}^{[m]}(X,L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times\iota^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,I)\times\mathrm{GW}^{[m]}(X,L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}I)\times\mathrm{GW}^{[m]}(Z,\iota^{\ast}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+m]}(X,I\otimes L)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+m]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}I\otimes\iota^{\ast}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+m]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}(I\otimes L))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}}

commutes up to homotopy. In a formula, we have that

\iota_{*}(\alpha\cup\iota^{*}\beta)=\iota_{*}(\alpha)\cup\beta

for any
\alpha\in\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}I)
and
\beta\in\mathrm{GW}^{[m]}_{j}(X,L)
.

Proof.

Our goal is to prove that the following diagram

\textstyle{\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Z)),\sharp_{\iota^{\Delta}I}\right)\times\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X)),\sharp_{L}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times(\iota^{\ast},\beta)}
\scriptstyle{(\iota_{\ast},\zeta)\times\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X)),\sharp_{I}\right)\times\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X)),\sharp_{L}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Z)),\sharp_{\iota^{\Delta}I}\right)\times\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(Z)),\sharp_{\iota^{\ast}L}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X)),\sharp_{I\otimes L}\right)}
\textstyle{\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Z)),\sharp_{\iota^{\Delta}I\otimes\iota^{\ast}L}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id},\theta)}
\textstyle{\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Z)),\sharp_{\iota^{\Delta}(I\otimes L)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\iota_{\ast},\zeta)}

of dg categories with duality, commutes up to the natural homotopy
\wp
. In other words, we need to show that the following diagram

\textstyle{\iota_{*}[A,\iota^{\Delta}I]\otimes[B,L]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\wp}
\textstyle{[\iota_{*}A\otimes B,I\otimes L]}
\textstyle{\iota_{*}([A,\iota^{\Delta}I]\otimes\iota^{*}[B,L])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta\theta\tau\beta}
\textstyle{[\iota_{*}(A\otimes\iota^{*}B),I\otimes L]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wp^{\sharp}}

commutes for
A\in\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Z))
and
B\in\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X))
, where
\tau
is defined in
[
Sch17
, (1.10)]
. By the commutativity of the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projection_residue}$}}
, the result follows from the commutativity of the diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 54.61287pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-40.15448pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise where
N\in\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Z))
. The commutativity of this diagram can be checked directly on sheaves or follows from
[
CH09
, Theorem 5.5.1]
.
∎

6.7.
Projection formula applied to regular immersions

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a vector bundle of rank
d
over a regular scheme
X
with a section
s:\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Assume that the closed embedding
\iota:Z\hookrightarrow X
of the zero locus
Z:=Z(s)
is a regular immersion of codimension
d
. Recall the Koszul complex
K(\mathcal{E})
given by the section
s
is

0\to\wedge^{d}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\to\wedge^{d-1}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\to\wedge^{d-1}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\to\cdots\to\wedge^{2}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{s}\mathcal{O}_{X}\to 0,

endowed with a symmetric form
\sigma:K(\mathcal{E})\to\big[K(\mathcal{E}),\ \wedge^{d}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}[d]\big]
on
K(\mathcal{E})
induced by the canonical pairing

\wedge^{i}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes\wedge^{d-i}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\to\wedge^{d}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}.

Note that the Koszul complex
K(\mathcal{E})
is acyclic off
Z
. Therefore, we have a canonical element
\kappa(\mathcal{E}):=(K(\mathcal{E}),\sigma)
in
\mathrm{GW}^{[d]}_{0}(X~\mathrm{on}~Z,\wedge^{d}\mathcal{E}^{\vee})
.

Remark 6.8
.

Note that the normal bundle
N_{\iota}
of the embedding is isomorphic to the bundle
\iota^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}
. On the one hand, the exact sequence
0\to\mathfrak{I}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z}\to 0
provides that
\mathfrak{I}/\mathfrak{I}^{2}\cong L^{1}\iota^{*}\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z}
. On the other hand, we have
L^{1}\iota^{*}\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z}\cong H^{1}(\iota^{*}K(\mathcal{E}))\cong\iota^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}
, since the Koszul complex
K(\mathcal{E})
provides a finite flat resolution, and the differentials in
\iota^{*}K(\mathcal{E})
vanish.

Consider the residue complex
E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee})
. By the fundamental local isomorphism (cf.
[
Har66
, Proposition III.7.2, p.179]
), we have that
\iota^{\Delta}E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee})[d]\cong\mathcal{O}_{Z}
in the derived category
D^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))
. Thus, we get a trivial symmetric form

1_{Z}:\mathcal{O}_{Z}\to\iota^{\Delta}E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee})[d]

in
\mathrm{GW}^{[d]}_{0}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee}))
.

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
L
be a line bundle on
X
. The following diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(X,L\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa(\mathcal{E})\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}_{Z}\left(X,\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes L\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}_{Z}(X,E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee})\otimes L)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\iota^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1_{Z}\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee})\otimes\iota^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}(E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee})\otimes L))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}}

commutes up to homotopy. In a formula, we have that

\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(\alpha)=\kappa(\mathcal{E})\cup\alpha

for any
\alpha\in\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(X,L)
.

Proof.

By the projection formula (cf. Proposition
6.6
), the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(X,L\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}(1_{Z})\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}_{Z}(X,L\otimes E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee}))}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\iota^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1_{Z}\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(Z,\iota^{*}L\otimes\iota^{\Delta}E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}(L\otimes E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}}

commutes. Note that
\iota_{*}(1_{Z})\simeq\rho(\kappa(\mathcal{E}))
in
\pi_{0}\mathrm{GW}^{[d]}_{Z}(X,E(\det\mathcal{E}^{\vee}))
, cf.
[
CH11
, Proposition 7.1]
. The result follows.
∎

6.10.
Some formulas in the regular case

Let
f:X\to Y
be a smooth morphism between regular schemes of relative dimension
d
. Define the relative canonical bundle
\omega_{X/Y}:=\mathrm{det}\,\Omega_{X/Y}
. Let
L
be a line bundle on
Y
. Then, the map
\rho:L\to EL
induces a stable equivalence

\rho:\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,L)\rightarrow\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,EL).

Note that, according to
[
Har66
, Theorem VI.3.1(d), p.318]
, there exists a resolution
\rho^{\prime}
given by the composition

\rho^{\prime}:f^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{X/Y}[d]\xrightarrow{\rho}E\left(f^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{X/Y}[d]\right)\cong Ef^{\sharp}L\xrightarrow{\rho}Ef^{\sharp}EL\xrightarrow{\cong}f^{\Delta}EL.

Recall that
f^{\sharp}=Lf^{\ast}(-)\otimes\omega_{X/Y}[d]
. Consequently,
\rho^{\prime}
induces a stable equivalence

\rho^{\prime}:\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(X,f^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{X/Y})\rightarrow\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,f^{\Delta}EL).

Definition 6.11
.

The pushforward map

f_{\ast}:\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(X,f^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{X/Y})\rightarrow\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,L)

of a smooth morphism
f
in the regular case is defined as the composite

\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(X,f^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{X/Y})\xrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,f^{\Delta}EL)\xrightarrow{f_{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,EL)\xrightarrow{\rho^{-1}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,L).

Proposition 6.12
.

Let

\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{g}}
\scriptstyle{\bar{f}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Y}

be a fibre product of regular schemes, where
f
is smooth of relative dimension
d
and
g
is an open immersion. Then,

g^{\ast}f_{\ast}\simeq\bar{f}_{\ast}\bar{g}^{\ast}

as maps from
\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(X,f^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{X/Y})
to
\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y^{\prime},g^{\ast}L)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.2
and Proposition
6.3
.
∎

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
X
be a regular scheme, and let
L,L^{\prime}
be line bundles on
X
. Let
\mathcal{E}
be a vector bundle over
X
of rank
r+1
, and let
p:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\to X
be the associated projective bundle. Then we have

p_{*}(\alpha\cup p^{*}\beta)=p_{*}(\alpha)\cup\beta

for any
\alpha\in\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),p^{\ast}L\otimes\mathcal{O}(-r-1))
and
\beta\in\mathrm{GW}^{[m]}_{j}(X,L^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Recall that the pushforward
p_{*}:\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),p^{\Delta}EL)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(X,EL)
is defined to be the composition

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),p^{\Delta}EL)\xleftarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathcal{A}_{p},\sharp_{p^{\Delta}EL})\xrightarrow{f_{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(X,EL)

cf. Section
3
. Take any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
. By the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{p}
(cf. Definition
3.10
), the canonical morphism
p_{\ast}A\to\mathrm{R}p_{\ast}A
is an isomorphism in
D^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))
. Then for any
B\in\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X))
, the following diagram

\textstyle{p_{\ast}(A\otimes p^{\ast}B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wp}
\textstyle{p_{\ast}A\otimes B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}p_{\ast}(A\otimes p^{\ast}B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wp_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R}p_{\ast}A\otimes B}

commutes by
[
Lip09
, (3.2.1.2), (3.1.2.3) and Proposition 3.2.4]
, where
\wp
is the isomorphism defined in (
15
), and
\wp_{1}
is the quasi-isomorphism given by
[
Lip09
, Proposition 3.9.4]
. The right vertical map is also a quasi-isomorphism, since
B
is flat. Therefore
A\otimes p^{\ast}B\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
, i.e. the image of the following composition

\mathcal{A}_{p}\times\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times p^{\ast}}\mathcal{A}_{p}\times\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(Z))\xrightarrow{\cup}\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))

of dg functor lies in
\mathcal{A}_{p}
. Our goal turns to show that the following diagram

\textstyle{\left(\mathcal{A}_{p},\sharp_{p^{\Delta}EL}\right)\times\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X)),\sharp_{L^{\prime}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\cup(p^{\ast},\beta)}
\scriptstyle{(p_{\ast},\zeta)\times\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X)),\sharp_{EL}\right)\times\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X)),\sharp_{L^{\prime}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{\left(\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X)),\sharp_{EL\otimes L^{\prime}}\right)}
\textstyle{\left(\mathcal{A}_{p},\sharp_{p^{\Delta}EL\otimes p^{\ast}L^{\prime}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id},\theta)}
\textstyle{\left(\mathcal{A}_{p},\sharp_{p^{\Delta}(EL\otimes L^{\prime})}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(p_{\ast},\zeta)}

of dg categories with duality, commutes up to the natural homotopy
\wp:p_{*}A\otimes B\to p_{*}(A\otimes p^{*}B)
for any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{p},B\in\textnormal{Ch}^{b}(\mathbf{V}(X))
.
By the proof of Proposition
6.6
, the result follows from Lemma
6.14
below.
∎

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a vector bundle over
X
of rank
r+1
, and let
p:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\to X
be the associated projective bundle.
Then, the following diagram

\textstyle{p_{\ast}(p^{\Delta}I\otimes p^{\ast}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wp^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{p_{\ast}p^{\Delta}I\otimes L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Tr}}
\textstyle{p_{\ast}p^{\Delta}(I\otimes L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Tr}}
\textstyle{I\otimes L.}

commutes in
Z^{0}\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))
for any dualizing complex
I
on
X
and any line bundle
L
on
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

By
[
Har66
, Proposition III.4.4, p.158]
(and
[
Con00
, Theorem 2.3.2]
for clarification), the diagram commutes in derived categories
D^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))
. Since
I\otimes L
is degreewise injective and bounded, if there is no homotopy between complexes
p_{\ast}(p^{\Delta}I\otimes p^{\ast}L)
and
I\otimes L
, then

\textnormal{Hom}_{Z^{0}\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))}(p_{\ast}(p^{\Delta}I\otimes p^{\ast}L),I\otimes L)\cong\textnormal{Hom}_{D^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))}(p_{\ast}(p^{\Delta}I\otimes p^{\ast}L),I\otimes L),

and the result follows, cf.
[
Wei94
, Corollary 10.4.7]
.
Under isomorphism
\wp
, it suffices to show that
\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathbf{M}(X)}((p_{\ast}p^{\Delta}I)^{p},I^{p-1})=0
for any
p\in\mathbb{Z}
. Take any morphism
t\in\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathbf{M}(X)}((p_{\ast}p^{\Delta}I)^{p},I^{p-1})
. Denote by
\mu:X\to\mathbb{Z}
and
\mu^{\prime}:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathbb{Z}
the codimensions associated with
I
and
p^{\Delta}I
respectively. Suppose that
t\neq 0
. Then there exists
r\in\Gamma(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),(p^{\Delta}I)^{p})
such that
0\neq t(p_{\ast}(r))\in I^{p-1}
. The support of
r
is a finite union
\overline{\{x_{1}\}}\cup\ldots\cup\overline{\{x_{n}\}}
with
\mu^{\prime}(x_{i})=p
. Then the support of
t(p_{\ast}(r))
is contained in
\overline{\{p(x_{1})\}}\cup\ldots\cup\overline{\{p(x_{n})\}}
, and
\mu(p(x_{i}))\geq p
by
[
Har66
, Proposition VI.3.4, p.333]
(and
[
Con00
, (3.1.25)]
for clarification), which is a contradiction.
∎

Let
\iota:Z\to X
be the morphism with the same conditions as presented in Subsection
6.7
. Set
\omega_{Z/X}=\mathrm{det}\,\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{E}\cong\iota^{\ast}\mathrm{det}\,\mathcal{E}
. Let
L
be a line bundle on
X
, then there is a stable equivalence

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,L)\xrightarrow{\rho}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,EL).

By the fundamental local isomorphism (cf.
[
Har66
, Proposition III.7.2, p.179]
), we have that
\iota^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{Z/X}[-d]\cong\iota^{\Delta}EL
in the derived category
D^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(Z))
.
Thus there is a resolution
\rho^{\prime}:\iota^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{Z/X}[-d]\to\iota^{\Delta}EL
, hence a stable equivalence

\rho^{\prime}:\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(Z,\iota^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{Z/X})\xrightarrow{}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}EL).

Then we define pushforward map

\iota_{\ast}:\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(Z,\iota^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{Z/X})\xrightarrow{}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,L).

of regular immersion
\iota
in the regular case as the composite

\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(Z,\iota^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{Z/X})\xrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\iota^{\Delta}EL)\xrightarrow{\iota_{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,EL)\xrightarrow{\rho^{-1}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,L).

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
\iota:Z\to X
be the regular immersion as above, and let
L
be a line bundle on
X
, then the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa(\mathcal{E})\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(X,\mathrm{det}\,\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes L)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\iota^{\ast}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\iota^{\ast}\left(\mathrm{det}\,\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes L\right)\otimes\omega_{Z/X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}}

commutes.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.9
.
∎

7.
Geometric description of the connecting homomorphism

In this section, we incorporate the assumption presented by Balmer and Calmès
[
BC09
]
together with Appendix
A
to investigate the connecting homomorphism in Hermitian
K
-theory.

7.1.
Hypothesis

Consider a regular immersion
\iota:Z\hookrightarrow X
of codimension
c\geq 2
. Let
\pi:B\to X
be the blow-up of
X
along
Z
, and
E
be the exceptional fibre. Define
U:=X-Z\cong B-E
as the open complement. We assume that
v:U\hookrightarrow X
and
\tilde{v}:U\hookrightarrow B
are corresponding embeddings. Suppose that there exists a scheme
Y
and an auxiliary flat morphism
\tilde{\alpha}:B\to Y
such that
\alpha:=\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{v}
forms an affine bundle, i.e., a Zariski locally trivial bundle. This setup can be illustrated in the following diagram:

\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}}
\textstyle{Y.}

7.2.
The main theorem

Let
L
be a line bundle on a regular scheme
X
. Under the isomorphism

\mathrm{Pic}(B)\cong\mathrm{Pic}(X)\oplus\mathbb{Z},

we have

\tilde{\alpha}^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}(L)\cong\pi^{*}(L)\otimes\mathcal{O}(E)^{\otimes\lambda(L)}

for some integer
\lambda(L)
(cf.
[
BC09
, Remark 2.2]
). Let
L_{Y}:=(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}(L)
. The aim of this section is to prove the following result.

Theorem 7.3
.

Suppose that
Z,X
are both regular schemes and that Hypothesis
7.1
holds.

(A)

If
\lambda(L)\equiv c-1\mod 2
, then we have

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,L)\simeq\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,L_{Y})\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-c]}(Z,\omega_{\iota}\otimes\iota^{\ast}L).

(B)

If
\lambda(L)\equiv c\mod 2
, then the following diagram

(17)

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,v^{\ast}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha^{*})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-c]}(Z,\omega_{\iota}\otimes\iota^{\ast}L)[1]}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n-c+1]}(Z,\omega_{\iota}\otimes\iota^{\ast}L)}
\scriptstyle{\eta\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,L_{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}^{*}\tilde{\alpha}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(E,\tilde{\iota}^{\ast}\tilde{\alpha}^{\ast}L_{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(E,\omega_{\widetilde{\pi}}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}^{*}(\omega_{\iota}\otimes\iota^{\ast}L))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{*}}

is commutative up to homotopy. Note that
\eta
is the Bott element defined in
[
Sch17
, (6.1)]
.

Proof.

(A) In view of the localization sequence

\mathrm{GW}^{[n-c]}(Z,\omega_{\iota}\otimes\iota^{\ast}L)\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,L)\xrightarrow{v^{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,v^{\ast}L)\xrightarrow{\partial}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Z,\omega_{\iota}\otimes\iota^{\ast}L)[1],

it is enough to show that
v^{*}
has a right splitting. To find this splitting, we form a diagram as follows

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 18$}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,v^{*}L)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,\alpha^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}L)}
\scriptstyle{b}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,\omega_{\pi}\otimes\pi^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}(U,\tilde{v}^{*}(\omega_{\pi}\otimes\pi^{*}L))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,{\tilde{\alpha}}^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 19$}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,\tilde{v}^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}}^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}^{*}(\epsilon)}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,\alpha^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,{\tilde{\alpha}}^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(Y,(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

The squares
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_split_1}$}}
and
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_split_2}$}}
are commutative by the base change formula (cf. Proposition
6.12
) and the functoriality of pullback respectively, and the isomorphisms
a
and
\gamma
are fixed by these properties. The isomorphism
\epsilon
can be chosen to be the following composition

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,\mathcal{O}(E)^{\otimes\lambda(L)}\otimes\pi^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,\mathcal{O}(E)^{\otimes(c-1)}\otimes\pi^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id},\rho_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,{\tilde{\alpha}}^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{id},\rho_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,\omega_{\pi}\otimes\pi^{*}L)}

where
\psi
is the two-periodicity on the twists, and
\rho_{0}
(resp.
\rho_{1}
) is the isomorphism
{\tilde{\alpha}}^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}v^{*}L\cong\mathcal{O}(E)^{\otimes\lambda(L)}\otimes\pi^{*}L
(resp.
\mathcal{O}(E)^{\otimes(c-1)}\otimes\pi^{*}L\cong\omega_{\pi}\otimes\pi^{*}L
), cf.
[
BC09
, Proposition A.11]
.
Then, we construct
b
to be the composition
\gamma\tilde{v}^{*}(\epsilon)a^{-1}
. It is more difficult to prove (B), and we postpone this case to Section
7.7
below. ∎

Remark 7.4
.

In Theorem
7.3
(B), compared to the work of Balmer-Calmès
[
BC09
]
, the additional data of cupping the Bott element become necessary to provide a geometric description of the connecting homomorphism in Hermitian
K
-theory.

7.5.
On the codimension one case

Let
B
be a scheme with a line bundle
L
and
\tilde{\iota}:E\hookrightarrow B
be a prime divisor, and
U:=B-E
be its open complement. Let
\tilde{v}:U\to B
be the open immersion. Let
\mathcal{O}(E)
be the line bundle on
B
associated to
E
. There is a canonical morphism of line bundles

\sigma_{E}:\mathcal{O}(E)^{\vee}\to\mathcal{O}_{B}.

Note that the morphism
\sigma_{E}
induces an isomorphism
t:\tilde{v}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}(E)\xrightarrow{\cong}\tilde{v}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{B}
. By Theorem
2.16
, there is a distinguished triangle

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{E}\left(B,L\right)\xrightarrow{}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(B,L\right)\xrightarrow{\tilde{v}^{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(U,\tilde{v}^{\ast}L\right)\xrightarrow{\partial}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{E}\left(B,L\right)[1].

in
\mathcal{SH}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

The following diagram of Grothendieck-Witt spectra

\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,L\otimes\mathcal{O}(E))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(E,\tilde{\iota}^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\tilde{\iota}}\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}_{\ast}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}_{E}(B,L)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta\cup}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(U,\tilde{v}^{\ast}\left(L\otimes\mathcal{O}(E)\right)\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(U,\tilde{v}^{\ast}L\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{E}(B,L)}[1]}

commutes up to homotopy.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the dg category
\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{V}(B))
. We define the set
w\subset Z^{0}\mathcal{A}
such that
\phi\in w
if and only if
\tilde{v}^{\ast}(\phi)\in\mathrm{quis}
. Note that
\mathrm{quis}\subset w
since the pullback of a quasi-isomorphism on
B
via

remains a quasi-isomorphism on
U
. Additionally, the morphism
\sigma_{E}
belongs to
w
. Consider the full subcategory
\mathrm{Fun}_{w}([1],\mathcal{A})
of
\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{A})
. It consists of functors
[1]\to\mathcal{A}
whose images are in
w
(see
[
Sch10
, p.367]
). Inside
\mathrm{Fun}_{w}([1],\mathcal{A})
, we have an object
\sigma_{E}
equipped with a symmetric form:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(E)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{E}}
\scriptstyle{1}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(E)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{E}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{B}}

with respect to the duality
\sharp_{\mathcal{O}(E)^{\vee}}:\mathrm{Fun}_{w}([1],\mathcal{A})^{\textnormal{op}}\to\mathrm{Fun}_{w}([1],\mathcal{A})
. Now, let us examine the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(E,\tilde{\iota}^{\ast}\left(L\otimes\mathcal{O}(E)\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 20$}}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(E,\tilde{\iota}^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\tilde{\iota}}\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}_{\ast}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,L\otimes\mathcal{O}(E))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\textnormal{cone}(\sigma_{E})\cup}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 21$}}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{E}\cup}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}_{E}(B,L)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta\cup}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 22$}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Fun}_{w}([1],\mathcal{A}),\sharp_{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{cone}}
\scriptstyle{P}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 23$}}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(U,\left(L\otimes\mathcal{O}(E)\right)|_{U}\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}\left(\mathcal{A},w,\sharp_{L}\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{E}(B,L)[1]}}

where the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_codi_1}$}}
is commutative up to homotopy, as stated in Lemma
6.15
. The commutativity of the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_codi_2}$}}
follows from
[
Bal05
, Theorem 5.2]
. The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_codi_3}$}}
is commutative since the trivialization
t
is induced by
\sigma_{E}
via
\tilde{v}^{*}
. Lastly, the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_codi_4}$}}
is commutative by Lemma
A.1
.
∎

7.7.
Proof of Theorem

(B)

Consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,v^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 24$}}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}_{Z}^{[n]}(X,L)[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 25$}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(Z,\iota^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\iota})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,\tilde{v}^{*}\left(\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi}\right))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 26$}}}

\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n-1]}(E,\tilde{\iota}^{\ast}\left(\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi}\right)\otimes\omega_{\tilde{\iota}})[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,\mathcal{O}(E)\otimes\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(E,\tilde{\iota}^{\ast}\left(\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi}\right)\otimes\omega_{\tilde{\iota}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta\cup}

The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_conn_2}$}}
commutes due to the composition of pushforwards. The commutativity of the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_conn_3}$}}
follows from Lemma
7.6
and the projection formula (cf. Proposition
6.6
) applied to the Bott elements. Lastly, the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:conn_conn_1}$}}
commutes by Lemma
7.8
below. Note that
\widetilde{\pi}_{\ast}
commutes with cupping Bott element by Proposition
6.13
. Then (
17
) commutes since
\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{v}=\alpha
and

\tilde{\alpha}^{\ast}L_{Y}=\pi^{*}(L)\otimes\mathcal{O}(E)^{\otimes\lambda(L)}=\mathcal{O}(E)^{\otimes(\lambda(L)-c-1)}\otimes\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi}=\mathcal{O}(E)\otimes\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi}

in
\mathrm{Pic}(B)/2
.

Lemma 7.8
.

There is a map of homotopy fibration sequences

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{E}(B,\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(B,\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,\tilde{v}^{*}\left(\pi^{\ast}L\otimes\omega_{\pi}\right))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}_{Z}^{[n]}(X,L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U,v^{*}L)}

Proof.

Denote by
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{\pi}
the full dg subcategory of
\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(B))
as defined in Definition
3.10
. Denote by
\mathcal{B}=\textnormal{Ch}^{b}_{c}(\mathbf{M}(X))
. Let
w\subset Z^{0}\mathcal{B}
(resp.
\tilde{w}\subset Z^{0}\mathcal{A}
) be the set of morphisms which are quasi-isomorphism after pullback along
v
(resp.
\tilde{v}
). Note that there is a cartesian square

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces U.}
\scriptstyle{v}

Then by base change theorem,
\pi_{\ast}(\tilde{w})\subset w
.
Thus
(\pi_{\ast},\zeta)
is a non-singular exact form functor between squares of dg categories with weak equivalences and duality

\textstyle{(\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{w}},\textnormal{quis})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{A},\textnormal{quis})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{B}^{w},\textnormal{quis})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{B},\textnormal{quis})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{w}},\tilde{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{A},\tilde{w})}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{B}^{w},w)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{B},w)}

Then
(\pi_{\ast},\zeta)
induces a map of homotopy fibre sequences of Grothendieck-Witt spectra.
∎

8.
Excess intersection formula

8.1.
Euler class

Let
L
be a line bundle on a regular scheme
X
. Let
p:V\to X
be a vector bundle of rank
d
and let
s:X\to V
be the zero section. The
Euler class map
of
V
is the composition of maps of spectra

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(X,{L})\xrightarrow{s_{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}({V},p^{*}(\det{V}^{\vee}\otimes{L}))\xrightarrow{(p^{*})^{-1}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n+d]}(X,\det{V}^{\vee}\otimes{L})

which shall be denoted by
c({V})_{L}
. From Projection Formula (Proposition
6.6
), we see that

(27)

c(V)_{L}(\alpha)=(p^{*})^{-1}s_{*}(1\cup s^{*}p^{*}(\alpha))=(p^{*})^{-1}(s_{*}(1)\cup p^{*}(\alpha))=[(p^{*})^{-1}s_{*}(1)]\cup\alpha,

for
\alpha\in\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(X,L)
, where the last equality follows from the fact that the pullback is compatible with the cup product (See the line before
[
Sch17
, Remark 9.4]
). Thus, the key information of the morphism
c(V)_{L}
is determined by the image of
1\in\mathrm{GW}_{0}(X)
.

Definition 8.2
.

The Euler class
e(V)
of
V
is defined to be the image of the element
1_{X}\in\mathrm{GW}_{0}(X)
under the composition of maps

\mathrm{GW}_{0}(X)\xrightarrow{s_{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[d]}_{0}({V},p^{*}\det{V}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{(p^{*})^{-1}}\mathrm{GW}^{[d]}_{0}(X,\det{V}^{\vee})

That is we define
e(V):=(p^{*})^{-1}s_{*}(1_{X})
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
X,Y
be regular schemes and let
p:V\to X
be a vector bundle of rank
d
.

(i)

If
f:Y\to X
is a morphism of schemes, then
e(f^{*}V)=f^{*}e(V)
.

(ii)

If
0\to V^{\prime}\to V\to V^{\prime\prime}\to 0
is an exact sequence of vector bundles over
X
, then
e(V)=e(V^{\prime\prime})\cup e(V^{\prime})
.

Proof.

See
[
Fas09
, Proposition 2.4]
.
∎

Remark 8.4
.

By
[
Fas09
, Proposition 2.5]
, we have
\kappa(V)=p^{*}e(V)
. Since
ps=\mathrm{id}
,
s^{\ast}\kappa(V)=e(V)
. Thus our definition of Euler class coincide with the definition in
[
Fas09
, Section 2.5]
.

8.5.
Base change

Consider the following fibre product

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\pi}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}

of regular schemes, where
\iota
is a regular immersion of codimension
c
, and
\pi:Z\to Y
is the zero locus of a section
s:\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{O}_{Y}
, where
\mathcal{E}
is a vector bundle on
Y
of rank
d
. Suppose that
Z
meets
X
properly. Let
N_{Y}X
denote the normal cone to
X
in
Y
.

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
L
be a line bundle on
Y
. Then, for any
\alpha\in\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(X,\det N_{Y}X\otimes\iota^{\ast}L)
, we have

\tilde{\iota}_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}(\alpha)=\pi^{\ast}\iota_{\ast}(\alpha)

in
\mathrm{GW}^{[n+c]}_{i}(Z,\pi^{\ast}L)
.

Proof.

Note that
\widetilde{\pi}:V\to X
is the zero locus of the section
\iota^{\ast}(s):\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{E}\to\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
, and
\kappa(\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{E})=\iota^{\ast}\kappa(\mathcal{E})
in
\mathrm{GW}^{[d]}_{0}(X,\det\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{\vee})
. Since
\pi_{\ast}:\mathrm{GW}^{[n+c]}(Z,f^{\ast}L)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n+c+d]}_{Z}(Y,L\otimes\det\mathcal{E})
is an equivalence by Dévissage Theorem
2.15
, it suffices to prove

\pi_{\ast}\pi^{\ast}\iota_{\ast}(\alpha)=\pi_{\ast}\tilde{\iota}_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}(\alpha).

On the left-hand side, by Lemma
6.15
, we have
\pi_{\ast}\pi^{\ast}\iota_{\ast}(\alpha)=\kappa(\mathcal{E})\cup\iota_{\ast}(\alpha)
. On the right-hand side,
\pi_{\ast}\tilde{\iota}_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}(\alpha)=\iota_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}_{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}(\alpha)=\iota_{\ast}(\kappa(\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{E})\cup\alpha)
. Then by the projection formula (Proposition
6.6
), we have
\iota_{\ast}(\kappa(\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{E})\cup\alpha)=\iota_{\ast}(\iota^{\ast}\kappa(\mathcal{E})\cup\alpha)=\kappa(\mathcal{E})\cup\iota_{\ast}\alpha
. The result follows.
∎

8.7.
Deformation to the normal cone

Consider a regular immersion
f:X\to Y
of regular schemes of codimension
c
. Denote by
N_{Y}X
the normal cone to
X
in
Y
and by
D(X,Y)
the deformation to the normal cone space, as discussed in
[
Nen07
, Section 3]
. Define
s:X\to N_{X}Y
as the embedding corresponding to the zero section. There exists a closed immersion
\mu:X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to D(X,Y)
and a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\delta}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\delta}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{N_{Y}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{0}}
\textstyle{D(X,Y)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces Y,}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{1}}

whose square are fibre products.

Lemma 8.8
.

The following maps

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{X}(N_{Y}X,\delta_{0}^{\ast}L)\xleftarrow{\delta_{0}^{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}(D(X,Y),L)\xrightarrow{\delta_{1}^{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{X}(Y,\delta_{1}^{\ast}L)

are stable equivalences for any line bundle
L
over
D(X,Y)
.

Proof.

The result follows from the Karoubi induction, cf.
[
Sch17
, Lemma 6.4]
. The
K
-theory case is proved in
[
Pan03
, Section 2.2.7]
, and the Witt theory case has been proved by
[
Nen07
]
.
∎

The composition
\delta_{1}^{\ast}(\delta_{0}^{\ast})^{-1}
is denoted by
d(X,Y)
.

Proposition 8.9
.

For any
\alpha\in\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(X,\det N_{Y}X\otimes f^{\ast}L)
, we have

f_{\ast}(\alpha)=d(X,Y)s_{\ast}(\alpha),

in
\pi_{i}\mathrm{GW}^{[n+c]}_{X}(Y,L)
.

Proof.

This result can be derived from the proof of
[
Fas09
, Proposition 2.9]
and by invoking Proposition
8.6
.
∎

8.10.
Excess intersection formula

Consider the following fibre product of regular schemes:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\pi}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\pi}

Suppose that
\iota
(resp.
\tilde{\iota}
) is a regular immersion of codimension
c
(resp.
c^{\prime}
), and that
\pi
(resp.
\pi^{\prime}
) is an arbitrary morphism. Let
N_{Y}X
(resp.
N_{Z}V
) denote the normal cone to
X
in
Y
(resp.
V
in
Z
), and define
E=\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}N_{Y}X/N_{Z}V
.

Theorem 8.11
.

Given a line bundle
L
on
Y
, for any
\alpha\in\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(X,\det N_{Y}X\otimes\iota^{\ast}L)
, we have:

\pi^{\ast}\iota_{\ast}(\alpha)=\tilde{\iota}_{\ast}\left(e(E)\cup\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}(\alpha)\right)

in
\mathrm{GW}^{[n+c]}_{i}(Z,\pi^{\ast}L)
.

Proof.

Let
D(X,Y)
(resp.
D(V,Z)
) represent the deformation to the normal cone space associated to
\iota:X\to Y
(resp.
\tilde{\iota}:V\to Z
). This gives us a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{N_{X}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D(X,Y)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces Y}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{s}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}}
\textstyle{N_{Z}V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{N}}
\textstyle{D(V,Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{D}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\pi}

Here,
s:X\to N_{X}Y
and
\tilde{s}:V\to N_{Z}V
represent the embeddings as zero sections. Let
p:N_{X}Y\to X
and
\tilde{p}:N_{Z}V\to V
be the projections, leading to another commutative diagram:

\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{N_{X}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}}
\textstyle{N_{Z}V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{p}}

We can then refer to (
27
) to obtain:

\widetilde{\pi}_{N}^{\ast}s_{\ast}(\alpha)=\widetilde{\pi}_{N}^{\ast}p^{\ast}(e(N_{X}Y)\cup\alpha)=\tilde{p}^{\ast}\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}(e(N_{X}Y)\cup\alpha)=\tilde{p}^{\ast}(\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}e(N_{X}Y)\cup\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha).

According to Proposition
8.3
, we find:

\tilde{p}^{\ast}(\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}e(N_{X}Y)\cup\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha)=\tilde{p}^{\ast}(e(\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}N_{X}Y)\cup\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha)=\tilde{p}^{\ast}(e(E)\cup e(N_{Z}V)\cup\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha).

Also, by invoking (
27
) once more, we have:

\tilde{p}^{\ast}(e(E)\cup e(N_{Z}V)\cup\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha)=\tilde{p}^{\ast}e(E)\cup\tilde{p}^{\ast}(e(N_{Z}V)\cup\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha)=\tilde{p}^{\ast}e(E)\cup\tilde{s}_{\ast}(\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha).

Then, by applying the projection formula (cf. Proposition
6.6
), we arrive at:

\tilde{p}^{\ast}e(E)\cup\tilde{s}_{\ast}(\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha)=\tilde{s}_{\ast}(\tilde{s}^{\ast}\tilde{p}^{\ast}e(E)\cup(\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha))=\tilde{s}_{\ast}(e(E)\cup(\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha)).

Finally, using Proposition
8.9
, we conclude that:

\displaystyle\pi^{\ast}\iota_{\ast}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\pi^{\ast}d(X,Y)s_{\ast}(\alpha)=d(V,Z)\widetilde{\pi}_{N}^{\ast}s_{\ast}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=d(V,Z)\tilde{s}_{\ast}(e(E)\cup(\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}\alpha))=\tilde{\iota}_{\ast}\left(e(E)\cup\widetilde{\pi}^{\ast}(\alpha)\right).

The result follows.
∎

9.
Projective bundles

Let
\mathcal{E}\rightarrow S
be a vector bundle of rank
r+1
over a regular scheme
S
.
Let
q:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow S
be the projective bundle associated to the vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
. The sequence of vector bundles on
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}(-1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{q^{*}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is exact where
\mathcal{Q}
is called the canonical quotient bundle. Following the ideas of Fasel
[
Fas13
, Section 5]
, we make the following construction.

Definition 9.1
.

For any
m\geq 1
. Define the twisting map

\mu^{m}:K(S)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{O}(-m))

as the following composition

K(S)\xrightarrow{Hq^{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-m+1]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{O}(-1))\xrightarrow{c(\mathcal{O}(1))^{m-1}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{O}(-m)).

Then we define

\Theta_{\textnormal{even}}:\bigoplus_{2\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ even}}K_{i}(S)\xrightarrow{\sum t_{m}\mu^{m}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))\textnormal{\quad\quad}\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}}:\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K_{i}(S)\xrightarrow{\sum t_{m}\mu^{m}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{O}(1))

where
t_{m}
are the two-periodicity on the twists.

By
[
BC09
, Proposition A.9(i)]
, we have that the canonical bundle
\omega_{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})/S}
is isomorphic to
q^{\ast}\mathrm{det}\,\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})}(-r-1)
. The aim of this section is to prove the following results.

Theorem 9.2
.

Let
S
be a regular scheme.

(A)

Suppose that
r
is even. The map

(\Theta_{\textnormal{even}},q^{*}):\bigoplus_{2\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ even}}K_{i}(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(S)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))

is an isomorphism of abelian groups in the even twisted case.

(B)

Suppose that
r
is even.
The map

(\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}},c(\mathcal{Q})q^{*})\colon\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K_{i}(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}_{i}(S,\det\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{O}(1))

is an isomorphism of groups in the odd twisted case.

(C)

Suppose that
r
is odd. The map

\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}}\colon\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K_{i}(S)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{O}(1))

is an isomorphism of abelian groups in the odd twisted case.

(D)

Suppose that
r
is odd. The following sequence

(28)

\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{2\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ even}}K_{i}(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Theta_{\mathrm{even}},q^{*})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}_{i}(S,\det\mathcal{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\begin{pmatrix}0\\[3.0pt]
\eta\cup c(\mathcal{E})\end{pmatrix}}
\textstyle{\ldots}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}_{i-1}(S,\det\mathcal{E})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i-1}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))}
\scriptstyle{q_{*}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\bigoplus\limits_{2\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ even}}K_{i-1}(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i-1}(S)}
\scriptstyle{(\Theta,q^{*})}

of abelian groups is exact in the even twisted case.

We will prove Theorem
9.2
when the bundle
\mathcal{E}
is trivial, and then apply the Mayer-Vietoris theorem to conclude the global case.

9.3.
The trivial bundle case

Define
\mathbb{P}^{r}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{S}^{r+1})
. Let
\iota:\mathbb{P}^{r-1}\to\mathbb{P}^{r}
be a hyperplane, and let
v:U\to\mathbb{P}^{r}
be its open complement. Inside
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
we can choose a hyperplane
\mathbb{P}^{r-2}
, and we have a composition of closed embeddings
\mathbb{P}^{r-2}\to\mathbb{P}^{r-1}\to\mathbb{P}^{r}
, which we still denote by
\iota
for simplicity if no confusion occurs.

Theorem 9.4
.

Let
S
be a regular scheme.

(a)

The map

(\iota_{*},q^{*}):\mathrm{GW}^{[n-1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-1},\mathcal{O}(1))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(S)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r})

is a stable equivalence of spectra in
\mathcal{SH}
.

(b)

The map

(Hq^{\ast},\iota_{*}):K(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-2]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-2},\mathcal{O}(1))\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-1))

is a stable equivalence of spectra in
\mathcal{SH}
.

Proof.

(a) The localization sequence (cf. Theorem
2.16
) yields a distinguished triangle

\mathrm{GW}^{[n-1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-1},\mathcal{O}(1))\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r})\xrightarrow{v^{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(U)\xrightarrow{\partial}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-1},\mathcal{O}(1))[1]

in
\mathcal{SH}
where
\omega_{\iota}=\mathcal{O}(1)
. Note that
U\cong\mathbb{A}^{r}
and we can find a left splitting of
v^{*}
by the map
q^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}
as follows:

where

is an equivalence by the homotopy invariance, and the diagram

commutes by the composition of pullbacks. It follows that the map

induces a equivalence of spectra.

Lemma 9.5
.

The following diagram

\textstyle{K(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{m}}
\scriptstyle{q^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-m))}
\textstyle{K(\mathbb{P}^{r-m+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-m+1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-m+1},\mathcal{O}(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}}

commutes for any
1\leq m\leq r
. Hence, the formula
\iota_{*}\mu^{m-1}=\mu^{m}
holds.

Proof.

To show the result, we expand the diagram

\textstyle{K(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{q^{\ast}}
\textstyle{K(\mathbb{P}^{r})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-m+1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c(\mathcal{O}(1))^{m-1}}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-m))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(\mathbb{P}^{r-m+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-m+1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-m+1},\mathcal{O}(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 38$}}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-m)).}

We show the commutativity of the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proj_bundle_twist_1}$}}
by induction on
m
. When
m=2
, the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proj_bundle_twist_1}$}}
commutes by Lemma
6.15
.
The induction step follows from the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-m+1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c(\mathcal{O}(1))^{m-2}}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 39$}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-m+1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c(\mathcal{O}(1))}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-m))}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-m+1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-1},\mathcal{O}(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c(\mathcal{O}(1))^{m-2}}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-1},\mathcal{O}(-m+1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-m+1]}(\mathbb{P}^{r-m+1},\mathcal{O}(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}}

where the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:pullback_euler}$}}
commutes by Proposition
8.3
and pullbacks commute with products.
∎

Proposition 9.6
.

Suppose that the bundle
\mathcal{E}
is trivial.

(a)

Theorem
9.2

(A)
is true.

(b)

If
r
is even, the map

(\Theta,\iota_{\ast}):\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(S)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(1))

is an equivalence of spectra.

(c)

Theorem
9.2

(C)
is true.

(d)

If
r
is odd, the map

(\Theta,q^{*},\iota_{\ast}):\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ even}}K(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(S)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r})

is an equivalence of spectra.

Proof.

(c) We prove this formula by induction. By Theorem
9.4
(b), we have that the map
Hq^{*}:K(S)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}(1))
is an equivalence. Note that
\mu^{1}=Hq^{*}
by definition. The induction step follows from the formula
\iota_{*}\mu^{m-1}=\mu^{m}
(cf. Lemma
9.5
) and the composition of pushforward.

(a) We apply Theorem
9.4
(a). Then, we use the formula
\iota_{*}\mu^{m-1}=\mu^{m}
again together with (c) proved above.

(b) It follows directly from Theorem
9.4
(b) that the map

(Hq^{\ast},\iota_{*}):K(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-2]}(S)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}(1))

is an equivalence of spectra. We deduce the general case by inductively applying Theorem
9.4
(b), the formula
\iota_{*}\mu^{m-2}=\mu^{m}
and the composition of pushforward.

(d) If
r
is odd and the twist is even, this follows from (b), the formula
\iota_{*}\mu^{m-1}=\mu^{m}
and Theorem
9.4
(a). ∎

Proposition 9.7
.

Suppose that
r
is even. Then, the sequence

0\to\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K_{i}(S)\xrightarrow{\Theta_{\mathrm{odd}}}\mathrm{GW}_{i}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(1))\xrightarrow{q_{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}_{i}(S)\to 0

of abelian groups is split exact.

Proof.

We claim that the composition
q_{*}\mu^{1}
vanishes, that is the composition

K(S)\xrightarrow{Hq^{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-1))\xrightarrow{t}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-r-1))\xrightarrow{q_{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(S)

is homotopic to zero, where
t
is the two-periodicity on the twists.

Set
r^{\prime}=r/2
. Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{K(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{O}(-r^{\prime})\cup}
\scriptstyle{H}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 40$}}}
\textstyle{K(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-r-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{H}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 41$}}}
\textstyle{K(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(-r-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(S)}

where the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_space_H_t}$}}
commutes by Lemma
2.18
and the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_space_H_q}$}}
commutes by Lemma
3.15
. Then to show that
q_{*}\mu^{1}
vanishes, it suffices to prove that the following composition

K(S)\xrightarrow{q^{\ast}}K(\mathbb{P}^{r})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{O}(-r^{\prime})\cup}K(\mathbb{P}^{r})\xrightarrow{q_{\ast}}K(S)

is homotopic to zero, which is true by the projection formula (cf.
[
TT90
, Proposition 3.17]
) and the fact that
\mathrm{R}q_{\ast}(\mathcal{O}(-r^{\prime}))\cong 0
(cf.
[
EGA3
, Chapter III Proposition 2.1.15]
).

For all
m
such that
1\leq m\leq r
, we note that the composition
q_{\ast}\mu^{m}
is also trivial, as
q_{\ast}\mu^{m}=q_{*}\iota_{*}\mu^{1}=q_{*}\mu^{1}=0
. Since
q\iota=\mathrm{id}
, we see that
\iota_{*}
provides a right splitting of
q_{*}
. Therefore, in view of Proposition
9.6
(b), the sequence

0\to\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K_{i}(S)\xrightarrow{\Theta}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(1))\xrightarrow{q_{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}_{i}(S)\to 0

is split exact.
∎

Remark 9.8
.

Suppose that
r
is even. If we consider the projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
, the composition

\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K_{i}(S)\xrightarrow{\Theta}\mathrm{GW}_{i}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{O}(1))\xrightarrow{q_{*}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}_{i}(S,\det\mathcal{E})

is still trivial, which follows from the same argument as in Proposition
9.7
.

9.9.
The general non-split case

Consider the projective bundle
p:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O})\to X
. The canonical split exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\rightarrow 0
induces two closed embeddings

\iota:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{S})\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O})\textnormal{ and }\iota^{\prime}:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}).

Let
v:U\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O})
(resp.
v^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O})
) be the associated open complements of
\iota
(resp.
\iota^{\prime}
). Note that
U^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{E}
and
U\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})}(1)
. Let
\alpha\colon U\to\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
(resp.
\alpha^{\prime}\colon U^{\prime}\to S
) be the projection.

Proof of Theorem
9.2

(C)
.

By the Mayer-Vietoris theorem (cf.
[
Sch10
, Theorem 1]
), it suffices to prove this case locally when
\mathcal{E}
is trivial, i.e.
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\cong\mathbb{P}^{r}
, which follows from Proposition
9.6
(c).
∎

Proof of Theorem
9.2

(A)
.

By the localization theorem, the sequence

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\prime}_{\ast}}

is a distinguished triangle in

. It is enough to show that the diagrams

. The diagram

Proof of Theorem

.

)

and

. This setting satisfies Hypothesis

, and so by Theorem

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

consists of short split exact sequences in horizontal lines (note that
\tilde{v}^{*}
has a right splitting
\tilde{\alpha}^{*}(\alpha^{*})^{-1}
). The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proj_bundle_even_twsit_excess}$}}
commutes by the excess intersection formula (cf. Theorem
8.11
). The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proj_bundle_even_bese}$}}
commutes by the base change formula (cf. Lemma
6.12
).
Since the map
\pi
factors as the composition
B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times_{S}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{\wp}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S})

where
i
is a regular immersion and
\wp
is the projection, we see that
\pi_{\ast}\pi^{\ast}=\mathrm{Id}
. Moreover, we have that

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}\pi^{\ast}(y)

\displaystyle=\wp_{*}i_{*}i^{*}\wp^{*}(y)

\displaystyle\text{(Composition of pushforwards and pullbacks)}

\displaystyle=\wp_{*}i_{*}(1_{S}\cup i^{*}\wp^{*}(y))

\displaystyle\text{($1_{S}\in\mathrm{GW}_{0}^{[0]}(S)$ is the identity element)}

\displaystyle=\wp_{*}i_{*}(1_{S})\cup y

\displaystyle\text{(Projection formulas, cf. Proposition \ref{prop:projection_formula} and \ref{prop:projection_projective_space})}

\displaystyle=\pi_{*}(1_{S})\cup y

\displaystyle\text{(Composition of pushforwards)}

\displaystyle=y

\displaystyle\text{($\pi_{*}(1_{S})=1$, cf.\ \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{balmer2012witt}{}{}, Proposition 3.15 (a)]})}

It follows that
c(\mathcal{Q})q^{\ast}
is a right splitting of
q_{*}
, i.e.,
q_{\ast}c(\mathcal{Q})q^{\ast}=\mathrm{id}
. Therefore, the map

(\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}},c(\mathcal{Q})q^{*})\colon\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K_{i}(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}_{i}(S,\det\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}^{r},\mathcal{O}(1))

is an isomorphism by Proposition
9.7
. For the general case, the result follows from the Mayer-Vietoris theorem.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9.2

.

We want to show that the candidate triangle

(47)

\textstyle{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}[1]}

is a distinguished triangle in the stable homotopy category
\mathcal{SH}
of spectra, where

\mathbf{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})),\quad\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}=\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(S,\det\mathcal{E}),\quad\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}=\bigoplus_{2\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ even}}K(S)[1]\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(S)[1]

and

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=q_{\ast},\quad\mathbf{v}^{\prime}={\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\eta\cup c(\mathcal{E})\end{pmatrix}},\quad\mathbf{w}^{\prime}=\big(\Theta_{\mathrm{even}},q^{\ast}\big).

1) Let
p:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S})\to S
be the canonical projection. By Lemma
7.6
and the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proj_space_partial}$}}
, the following sequence

\textstyle{\mathbf{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{f}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{v}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{w}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}[1]}

is a distinguished triangle, where

\mathbf{X}=\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})),\quad\mathbf{Y}=\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S}),\mathcal{O}(1)),\quad\mathbf{Z}=\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}(S)

and

\mathbf{f}=\iota^{\prime}_{\ast},\quad\mathbf{v}=(\alpha^{\ast})^{-1}v^{\prime\ast},\quad\mathbf{w}=\eta\cup q^{\ast}.

2) Note that
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
, and we form the following diagram

(48)

\textstyle{\mathbf{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{f}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{g}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{v}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{w}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{h}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}

where

Note that

.

3) To show the candidate triangle (

) is a distinguished triangle, it is enough to prove that the diagram (

) is a map of candidate triangles and the cone of the diagram (
48
) is a distinguished triangle.

Sublemma 9.10
.

The diagram (
48
) is a map of candidate triangles.

Proof.

All we need to show is that
\mathbf{v^{\prime}}\mathbf{g}=\mathbf{h}\mathbf{v}
. Consider the following exact sequence:

0\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S})}(-1)\to p^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S})\to\mathcal{G}\to 0

with
\mathcal{G}
the universal quotient bundle. The diagrams

\textstyle{K(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{1}}
\scriptstyle{H}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 49$}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(S,\det\mathcal{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c(\mathcal{G})p^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{c(\mathcal{E})}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 50$}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S}),\mathcal{O}(1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{\prime\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}(U^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S}),\mathcal{O}(1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{\prime\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n+1]}(U^{\prime})}

commute. Note that
\mu^{1}=Hp^{\ast}
and
pv^{\prime}=\alpha^{\prime}
. Hence,
v^{\prime\ast}\mu^{1}\simeq H\alpha^{\prime\ast}\simeq\alpha^{\prime\ast}H
, which shows the commutativity of the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_bundle_odd_diag_2}$}}
. The commutativity of the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_bundle_odd_diag_3}$}}
follows from the proof of
[
HMX22
, Theorem A.7]
. Moreover, since
\eta\cup H=0
and
\alpha^{\prime*}
is an isomorphism, we obtain the following formula

\eta\cup\big(\alpha^{\prime\ast}\big)^{-1}v^{\prime\ast}(\mu^{1},c(\mathcal{G})p^{\ast})\simeq\eta\cup(H,c(\mathcal{E}))\simeq(0,\eta\cup c(\mathcal{E})).

Furthermore,
v^{\prime\ast}\mu^{m}=0
for
m\neq 1
odd by the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_iota_prime_HF_3}$}}
. It follows that

\eta\cup(\alpha^{\prime\ast})^{-1}v^{\prime\ast}\Theta_{\mathrm{odd}}=0.

In view of (B) and Remark
9.8
, we have
p_{\ast}\big(\Theta_{\mathrm{odd}},c(\mathcal{G})p^{\ast}\big)=(0,\mathrm{id})
. Therefore,

\eta\cup(\alpha^{\prime\ast})^{-1}v^{\prime\ast}\big(\Theta_{\mathrm{odd}},c(\mathcal{G})p^{\ast}\big)\simeq\big(0,\eta\cup c(\mathcal{E})\big)\simeq\eta\cup c(\mathcal{E})p_{\ast}\big(\Theta_{\mathrm{odd}},c(\mathcal{G})p^{\ast}\big).

By (B) the map
\big(\Theta_{\mathrm{odd}},c(\mathcal{G})p^{\ast}\big)

is an equivalence. Hence,
\eta\cup(\alpha^{\prime\ast})^{-1}v^{\prime\ast}=\eta\cup c(\mathcal{E})p_{\ast}
.
∎

\scriptstyle{\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
\eta\cup c(\mathcal{E})&\eta\cup\end{pmatrix}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\prime}_{\ast}\big(\Theta_{\mathrm{even}},q^{\ast}\big)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y}[1]}

where
\mathbf{E}=\bigoplus\limits_{2\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ even}}K(S)
and the sequence

\textstyle{\mathbf{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta\cup}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-F}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}[1]}

is the (shifted) algebraic Bott sequence

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.84721pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.84721pt\raise
