-

The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proejctive_bundle_D_1}$}}
commutes by (B) and the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_bundle_odd_diag_3}$}}
.

-

The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_bundle_D_4}$}}
commutes by the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_bundle_odd_diag_2}$}}
.

-

The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proejctive_bundle_D_6}$}}
commutes by the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_iota_prime_HF_1}$}}
.

-

The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proejctive_bundle_D_7}$}}
commutes by the diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_iota_prime_HF_3}$}}
and Remark
9.8
.

-

The diagram
{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:proejctive_bundle_D_9}$}}
commutes as
\iota^{\prime}_{\ast}\mu^{m}=\mu^{m+1}
.

-

Other diagrams commute by definition.

Finally, we form the sums of these three maps of candidate triangles

\textstyle{\mathbf{Y^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{A}\oplus\mathbf{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbf{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}\oplus\mathbf{E}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}[1]\oplus\mathbf{E}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\oplus\mathbf{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Y}[1]}

where all the vertical maps are equivalences (Note that the left vertical map is an equivalence by (B). The upper horizontal line is a distinguished triangle, and the result follows.
∎

Theorem
9.2
(D) follows from Sublemma
9.10
and
9.11
.
∎

Suppose that
\phi:S^{\prime}\to S
is a morphism of regular schemes. Then, we can form the following fibre product

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times_{S}S^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{q^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{S^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{S}

Note that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times_{S}S^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{P}(\phi^{*}\mathcal{E})
which is a projective bundle over
S^{\prime}
, cf.
[
GW20
, Remark 13.27 p. 383]
.
The following result is a direct application of the projective bundle theorem:

Proposition 9.12
.

For any line bundle
L
on
S
, the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\phi^{*}\mathcal{E}),\omega_{q^{\prime}}\otimes q^{\prime*}\phi^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{\prime}_{*}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\omega_{q}\otimes q^{*}L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime*}}
\scriptstyle{q_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(S^{\prime},\phi^{*}L)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}(S,L)}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}

of spectra commutes up to homotopy.

Proof.

Suppose that the rank of the bundle
\mathcal{E}
is odd (i.e.
r
is even). Since we have the isomorphism

(\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}},c(\mathcal{Q})q^{*})\colon\bigoplus_{1\leq m\leq r}^{m\textnormal{ odd}}K_{i}(S)\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-r]}_{i}(S,\det\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathcal{O}(1)),

it is enough to show that

q^{\prime}_{\ast}\phi^{\prime*}(\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}},c(\mathcal{Q})q^{*})=\phi^{*}q_{\ast}(\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}},c(\mathcal{Q})q^{*}).

Note that
\phi^{\prime*}\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}}=\Theta^{\prime}_{\textnormal{odd}}\phi^{*}
follows from the definition of
\Theta
. Therefore, we see that

q^{\prime}_{\ast}\phi^{\prime*}\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}}=q^{\prime}_{\ast}\Theta^{\prime}_{\textnormal{odd}}\phi^{*}=0=\phi^{*}q_{\ast}\Theta_{\textnormal{odd}}.

Moreover, we have that

q^{\prime}_{\ast}\phi^{\prime*}c(\mathcal{Q})q^{*}=q^{\prime}_{\ast}c(\phi^{\prime*}\mathcal{Q})\phi^{\prime*}q^{*}=q^{\prime}_{\ast}c(\phi^{\prime*}\mathcal{Q})q^{\prime*}\phi^{*}=\phi^{*}=\phi^{*}q_{\ast}c(\mathcal{Q})q^{*}.

Now, assume that the rank of bundle
\mathcal{E}
is even (i.e.
r
is odd). Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{P}(\phi^{*}\mathcal{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}(\phi^{*}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{p^{\prime}}
\textstyle{S^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{S}

where we have
q=\iota p
and
q^{\prime}=\iota^{\prime}p^{\prime}
. Note that the left square is tor-independent. It follows that

\phi^{*}q_{*}=\phi^{*}p_{*}\iota_{*}=p^{\prime}_{*}\phi^{\prime\prime*}\iota_{*}=p^{\prime*}\iota^{\prime}_{*}\phi^{\prime*}=q^{\prime}_{*}\phi^{\prime*}

where the second equation follows from the case when
r
is even, and the third equation is true by the excess intersection formula (cf. Theorem
8.11
).
∎

Corollary 9.13

(Base change formula for projective morphisms)
.

Let

\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{g}}
\scriptstyle{\bar{f}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Y}

be a fibre product of regular schemes. Suppose that
f
is flat and
g
is a projective morphism. Then, the formula

f^{*}g_{*}=\bar{g}_{*}\bar{f}^{*}

holds on the level of
\mathrm{GW}
-spectra.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
8.11
and Proposition
9.12
.
∎

10.
Grassmannians

Let
S
be a regular scheme. Let
\mathcal{V}
be a vector bundles over
S
of rank
r
. Following
[
BC12
]
, we adopt the notation
\mathcal{P}_{d}\lhd\mathcal{V}
to indicate that
\mathcal{P}_{d}
is a subbundle of
\mathcal{V}
of rank
d
which is locally a direct summand (i.e.
\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{P}_{d}
is a vector bundle), and we call
\mathcal{P}_{d}
an
admissible subbundle
of
\mathcal{V}
.

Definition 10.1

(
[
EGA71
, Theorem 9.7.4]
)
.

Let
d
be an integer satisfying
0\leq d\leq r
. The
Grassmann functor
is the functor

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})(-)\colon&\textnormal{Sch}_{S}&\to\textnormal{Sets}\\
&X&\mapsto\Big\{\mathcal{P}_{d}\lhd f^{*}\mathcal{V}\Big\}\end{array}

for every given
S
-scheme
f:X\to S
. The
S
-scheme representing the functor
\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})(-)
is denoted by
p:\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})\to S
and is called the
Grassmann variety
over
S
. The universal element of the Grassmann functor is given by a unique admissible subbundle
\mathcal{T}_{d}\lhd\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})}
called the
tautological bundle
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})
.
Define
m:=r-d
and let

\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(m):=\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus r}).

The determinant line bundle of the tautological bundle
\mathcal{T}_{d}
which is denoted by
\Delta_{d}:=\det(\mathcal{T}_{d})
plays an important role in our computation.

10.2.
The flag variety

Fix a complete flag

0=\mathcal{V}_{0}\lhd\mathcal{V}_{1}\lhd\ldots\lhd\mathcal{V}_{r}=\mathcal{V}

of admissible subbundles of
\mathcal{V}
with
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{V}_{s})=s
. Recall the following construction in
[
BC12
, Definition 1.6]
.

Definition 10.3
.

Given a pair of
k
-tuples
\underline{d}=(d_{1},\ldots,d_{k})
and
\underline{m}:=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{k})
such that

0<d_{1}<d_{2}<\ldots<d_{k}\quad\textnormal{ and }\quad d_{1}+m_{1}\leq d_{2}+m_{2}\leq\ldots\leq d_{k}+m_{k}.

Define
\underline{r}:=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{k})
where
r_{i}:=d_{i}+m_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,k
. Set

\mathcal{V}_{\bullet}:=\big\{\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}}\lhd\ldots\lhd\mathcal{V}_{r_{k}}\big\}

The
flag functor
is the functor

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})(-)\colon&\textnormal{Sch}_{S}&\to\textnormal{Sets}\\
&X&\mapsto\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})(X)\end{array}

defined by

\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})(X):={\left\{\begin{matrix}\mathcal{P}_{d_{1}}&\lhd&\mathcal{P}_{d_{2}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{P}_{d_{k}}\\
\triangle&&\triangle&&&&\triangle\\
f^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}}&\lhd&f^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{r_{2}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&f^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{r_{k}}\end{matrix}\right\}}

for any
S
-scheme
f:X\to S
. The
S
-scheme representing the functor
\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})(-)
is denoted by
p:\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})\to S
and is called the
flag variety
over
S
. If no confusion occurs, we shall drop the mention of
f^{\ast}
in the sequel. The universal element of the functor
\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})(-)
is given by

\begin{matrix}\mathcal{T}_{d_{1}}&\lhd&\mathcal{T}_{d_{2}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{T}_{d_{k}}\\
\triangle&&\triangle&&&&\triangle\\
p^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}}&\lhd&p^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{r_{2}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&p^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{r_{k}}.\end{matrix}

The determinant line bundle of the vector bundle
\mathcal{T}_{d_{i}}
is denoted by
\Delta_{d_{i}}:=\det(\mathcal{T}_{d_{i}})
for
1\leq i\leq k
.

Remark 10.4
.

Note that
\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})=\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(\mathcal{V})
. The flag variety
p:\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})\to S
is smooth over
S
(cf.
[
BC12
, Proposition 1.13]
).

Proposition 10.5
.

The map

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pic}(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq k}\mathbb{Z}\Delta_{d_{i}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet}))

\displaystyle(L,m_{1},m_{2},\ldots,m_{k})

\displaystyle\mapsto p^{\ast}L\otimes\bigotimes_{1\leq i\leq k}\Delta_{d_{i}}^{\otimes m_{i}}.

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

See
[
BC12
, Proposition 1.13]
.
∎

Remark 10.6
.

In particular, Proposition
10.5
states that
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))\cong\mathrm{Pic}(S)\oplus\mathbb{Z}\Delta_{d}
. Since
\mathrm{Pic}(S)
is a direct summand of
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))
, we may omit
p^{\ast}
and denote by
L
the corresponding class in
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))
for
L\in\mathrm{Pic}(S)
.

10.7.
Canonical maps between flag varieties

Suppose that we have another
k
-tuple
\underline{\tilde{r}}:=(\tilde{r}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{r}_{k})
such that
\tilde{r}_{1}\leq\ldots\leq\tilde{r}_{k}
and
r_{i}\leq\tilde{r}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
. Set
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\bullet}
to be the flag
\mathcal{V}_{\tilde{r}_{1}}\lhd\ldots\lhd\mathcal{V}_{\tilde{r}_{k}}
. Then, we have a regular closed embedding

\displaystyle\iota:\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle{\left\{\begin{matrix}\mathcal{P}_{d_{1}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{P}_{d_{k}}\\
\triangle&&&&\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{V}_{r_{k}}\end{matrix}\right\}}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\left\{\begin{matrix}\mathcal{P}_{d_{1}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{P}_{d_{k}}\\
\triangle&&&&\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}_{\tilde{r}_{1}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{V}_{\tilde{r}_{k}}\end{matrix}\right\}}.

Now, let
J=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{\widehat{k}}\}
be a subset of the set
I:=\{1,\ldots,k\}
. Consider the
\widehat{k}
-tuples
\underline{\widehat{d}}:=(d_{j_{1}},\ldots,d_{j_{\widehat{k}}})
. Set
\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{\bullet}
to be the flag
\mathcal{V}_{r_{j_{1}}}\lhd\ldots\lhd\mathcal{V}_{r_{j_{\widehat{k}}}}
. Then, one can construct a natural projective morphism

\displaystyle\pi:\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{\widehat{d}}}(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle{\left\{\begin{matrix}\mathcal{P}_{d_{1}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{P}_{d_{k}}\\
\triangle&&&&\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{V}_{r_{k}}\end{matrix}\right\}}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\left\{\begin{matrix}\mathcal{P}_{d_{j_{1}}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{P}_{d_{j_{\widehat{k}}}}\\
\triangle&&&&\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}_{r_{j_{1}}}&\lhd&\ldots&\lhd&\mathcal{V}_{r_{j_{\widehat{k}}}}\end{matrix}\right\}}.

Remark 10.8
.

If neccesary, we may decorate the notations
\iota
and
\pi
without defining them cumbersomely, e.g.
\iota_{+}
and
\pi_{+}
etc..

10.9.
The blow-up setup

Consider the regular closed embedding
\iota:\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})
, of which the open complement shall be denoted by
v:\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})\backslash\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})\to\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})
.
The scheme
\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})\backslash\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})
can also be interpreted as the non-vanishing locus of the composition
\mathcal{T}_{d}\to p^{*}\mathcal{V}\to p^{*}\mathcal{V}/p^{*}\mathcal{V}^{1}
. By forming the fibre square

\textstyle{p^{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{p^{\ast}\mathcal{V}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{d}\cap p^{*}\mathcal{V}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

we observe that the points of
\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})\backslash\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})
can be characterized by the set

\Big\{\mathcal{P}_{d}\lhd\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{P}_{d}\overline{\ntriangleleft}\mathcal{V}^{1}\Big\},

where
\mathcal{P}_{d}\overline{\ntriangleleft}\mathcal{V}^{1}
signifies
\mathcal{P}_{d}\cap\mathcal{V}^{1}\lhd\mathcal{V}^{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{d}\not\subset\mathcal{V}^{1}
. Recall the diagram from
[
BC12
, Section 5]
:

(57)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})\backslash\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{v}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{V}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1})}

where the maps are defined on the functors of points as follows:

\textstyle{{\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{d}\\
\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}^{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{d}\\
\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{d}&\overline{\ntriangleleft}\mathcal{V}^{1}\\
\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{d-1}&\lhd&\mathcal{P}_{d}\\
\triangle&&\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}^{1}&=&\mathcal{V}^{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{d-1}&\lhd&\mathcal{P}_{d}\\
\triangle&&\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}^{1}&\lhd&\mathcal{V}\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{d-1}\\
\triangle\\
\mathcal{V}^{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right\}}.}

Here
\tilde{\iota},\pi,\tilde{\alpha}
and
\widetilde{\pi}
are the canonical morphisms defined in Section
10.7
. The morphism
\tilde{v}
maps
\mathcal{P}_{d}
to the flag
\mathcal{P}_{d-1}\lhd\mathcal{P}_{d}
with
\mathcal{P}_{d-1}=\mathcal{P}_{d}\cap\mathcal{V}^{1}
. Moreover,
\alpha
is defined as
\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{v}
. The diagram (
57
) satisfies the assumption of Theorem
7.3
.

10.10.
Notations on line bundles

Let
L
be a line bundle over
S
.

Line bundle

Scheme

d
odd

d
even

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{d}^{(0)}=\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}

\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})

L

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}_{d}

\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})

\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1})

Notations of line bundles on the corresponding Grassmannians

Moreover, we introduce the following notations

\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{e}(\mathcal{V}^{j}):=(\mathrm{Gr}_{e}(\mathcal{V}^{j}),\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{e}^{(j)})\textnormal{ and }\mathbb{G}_{e}(\mathcal{V}^{j}):=(\mathrm{Gr}_{e}(\mathcal{V}^{j}),\mathcal{H}_{e}^{(j)})

for Grassmannians with fixed line bundles.

10.11.
The induction step

The subsequent theorem is crucial for the proof of Theorem
10.45
.

Theorem 10.12
.

Suppose that
d\geq 2
and
r\geq d+2
.

(A)

The map

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))\xleftarrow[\simeq]{\big(\iota_{\ast},\pi_{\ast}\tilde{\alpha}^{\ast}\big)}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))

is an equivalence of spectra.

(B)

There is an equivalence

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))\simeq K(\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-2d]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d-2}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))

of spectra.

In order to apply Theorem
7.3
to prove Theorem
10.12
, one needs to know the number
\lambda(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}})-d
and
\lambda(\mathcal{H})-d
in
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
. Recall that
\lambda(L)=0
and
\lambda(L\otimes\Delta_{d})=1
for any
L\in\mathrm{Pic}(S)
, cf.
[
BC12
, Remark 5.6]
.

Proof of Theorem
10.12

(A)
.

In this case, we have
\lambda(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}})\equiv d-1\mod 2
. Note that

\omega_{\iota}\cong(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{V}^{1})^{d}\otimes\Delta_{d}^{-1},

by
loc. cit.
. The result follows, since the homotopy fibration sequence

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha^{\ast})^{-1}v^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))}

splits with a right splitting given by
\pi_{\ast}\tilde{\alpha}^{\ast}
(cf. Theorem
7.3
(A)).
∎

Proof of Theorem
10.12

(B)
.

By the localization theorem, the following sequence

(58)

\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))\xrightarrow{\iota_{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))\xrightarrow{(\alpha^{\ast})^{-1}v^{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))\xrightarrow{\partial}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))[1]

is a distinguished triangle in
\mathcal{SH}
. Since
\lambda(\mathcal{H})\equiv d\mod 2
, we see that Theorem
7.3
(B) applies to this case. Therefore, the connecting homomorphism
\partial
can be interpreted geometrically by the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\pi}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))[1]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d+1]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta\cup}
\textstyle{\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{F}l_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{V}^{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Sublemma 10.13
.

.

Remark 10.14
.

If we do not pursue the explicit description of the additive basis, then Theorem
10.45
follows directly from Theorem
10.12
by means of induction. However, it requires more effort to construct the additive basis, which will occupy the rest of this section.

10.15.
On the map
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})

The purpose of this subsection is to describe the underlying map in Theorem
10.12
(B) explicitly using pushforwards and pullbacks. Let

\widehat{\pi}_{1}:\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\to\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2})\quad\quad\widehat{\pi}_{2}:\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\to\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})

\widecheck{\pi}_{1}:\mathrm{Fl}_{d-2,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\to\mathrm{Gr}_{d-2}(\mathcal{V}^{2})\quad\quad\widecheck{\pi}_{2}:\mathrm{Fl}_{d-2,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\to\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})

be the canonical morphisms. Then, we can form the following pushforwards:

\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{FL}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}))\xrightarrow{\widehat{\pi}_{2\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))\quad\quad\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{FL}_{d-2,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}))\xrightarrow{\widecheck{\pi}_{2\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))

where we define

\mathbb{FL}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})=(\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}),\omega_{\widehat{\pi}_{2}}\otimes\widehat{\pi}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{H})\quad\quad\mathbb{FL}_{d-2,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})=(\mathrm{Fl}_{d-2,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}),\omega_{\widecheck{\pi}_{2}}\otimes\widecheck{\pi}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{H}).

Lemma 10.16
.

Let
\widehat{\iota}:\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{2})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})
be the canonical embedding. Then, the map

(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h}):K(\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-2d]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d-2}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))\longrightarrow\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))

in the diagram (
70
) is an equivalence of spectra, if we let

\mathbf{f}=(\widehat{\pi}_{2})_{*}H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}\quad\quad\mathbf{g}=\widehat{\iota}_{*}\quad\quad\mathbf{h}=(\widecheck{\pi}_{2})_{*}(\widecheck{\pi}_{1})^{*}

where
H
is the hyperbolic map

(71)

H:K(\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}))\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{FL}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})).

Proof.

The diagram (
10.11
) commutes by the functoriallity of pushforward. Thus, it is enough to show the commutativity of the diagrams (
10.11
), (
10.11
) and (
10.11
). Form the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}_{1}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\widehat{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{v}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{+}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})\backslash\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}_{d-2,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widecheck{\pi}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\widecheck{\pi}_{2}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\widecheck{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widecheck{v}}
\scriptstyle{\widecheck{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\widecheck{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}_{d-2,d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}_{+}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d-2}(\mathcal{V}^{2})}

where all the diagrams are commutative and the four square diagrams are cartesian (cf.
[
BC12
, Proof of Lemma 5.7]
).

(i) The commutativity of the diagram (
10.11
) can be obtained by the following identites

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}(\pi_{+})_{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{+}^{*}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{\pi}_{*}\widecheck{\alpha}^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{+}^{*}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Base change formula)}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{\pi}_{*}\widecheck{v}^{*}(\widecheck{\pi}_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Composition of pullbacks)}

\displaystyle=v^{*}(\widecheck{\pi}_{2})_{*}(\widecheck{\pi}_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Base change formula)}

(ii) The commutativity of the diagram (
10.11
) follows from the following identites

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}(\iota_{+})_{*}H

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}_{*}\widehat{\alpha}^{*}H

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Base change formula)}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}_{*}H\widehat{\alpha}^{*}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Pullbacks commute with hyperbolic maps)}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}_{*}H\widehat{v}^{*}(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Composition of pullbacks)}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\pi}_{*}\widehat{v}^{*}H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Pullbacks commute with hyperbolic maps)}

\displaystyle=v^{*}(\widehat{\pi}_{2})_{*}H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Base change formula)}

Note that one source of confusion is that
H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}\neq(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}H
since the targets have different dualities, although
\widehat{v}^{*}H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}=\widehat{v}^{*}(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}H
.

(iii) To show the commutativity of the diagram (
10.11
), we form the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{-}}
\scriptstyle{\breve{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}_{-}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}_{1}}

Now, we see that

\displaystyle\iota_{*}(\pi_{-})_{*}(\tilde{\alpha}_{-})^{*}

\displaystyle=(\widehat{\pi}_{2})_{*}\breve{\iota}_{*}(\tilde{\alpha}_{-})^{*}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Composition of pullbacks)}

\displaystyle=(\widehat{\pi}_{2})_{*}H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*}F

\displaystyle\textnormal{(The diagram }{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle\ref{diag:projective_space_1}$}})

Note that in the last equality, we have identified the map
\breve{\iota}:\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}^{1})\to\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})
with the closed embedding of projective bundles
\iota:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{V}^{1}/\mathcal{T}_{d-1})\to\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{T}_{d-1})
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2})
.
∎

10.17.
Buffalo-check Young diagrams

Any
d
-tuple
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{d})
of integers such that

m\geq\Lambda_{1}\geq\Lambda_{2}\geq\ldots\geq\Lambda_{d}\geq 0

shall be called a
Young diagram
in
(d\times m)
-frame (cf.
[
BC12
, Definition 2.1]
). See Figure
1
in the introduction. The non-increasing sequence

m\geq\Lambda_{1}\geq\Lambda_{2}\geq\ldots\geq\Lambda_{d}\geq 0

can be rewritten uniquely as follows:

m\geq\Lambda_{1}=\ldots=\Lambda_{d_{1}}>\Lambda_{d_{1}+1}=\ldots=\Lambda_{d_{2}}>\ldots>\Lambda_{d_{k-1}+1}=\ldots=\Lambda_{d_{k}}\geq 0,

where
d_{k}=d
.
One can form pairs of
k
-tuples of integers

(72)

\underline{d}=(d_{1},\ldots,d_{k})\quad\textnormal{ and }\quad\underline{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}),

where
m_{i}:=m-\Lambda_{d_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq k
. Set
d_{0}=m_{0}=0
. Define another pair of
k
-tuples

\underline{v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{k})\quad\textnormal{ and }\quad\underline{h}=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{k}),

where
v_{i}=d_{i}-d_{i-1}
and
h_{i}=m_{i}-m_{i-1}
for
1\leq i\leq k
.
Construct a
2k+1
-tuple

\underline{s}=(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{2k+1})=\begin{cases}(\Lambda_{d},v_{k},h_{k},v_{k-1},h_{k-1}\ldots,v_{2},h_{2},v_{1},h_{1})&\textnormal{if $\Lambda_{d}>0$}\\
(v_{k},h_{k},v_{k-1},h_{k-1}\ldots,v_{2},h_{2},v_{1},h_{1},0)&\textnormal{if $\Lambda_{d}=0$}\end{cases}

Note that the number of segements is precisely the number of non-zero terms in
\underline{s}
, and
s_{i}
is the length of the
i
-th segments provided it is non-zero (cf. the introduction). One may find
[
BC12
, Figure 3, page 611]
helpful.

Definition 10.18
.

A Young diagram
\Lambda
in a
(d\times m)
-frame is called
K
-even
if one of the following conditions holds:

(i)

\Lambda_{d_{k}}=0
;

(ii)

there exists an integer
r(\Lambda)\geq 2
such that
s_{2},\ldots,s_{r(\Lambda)-1}
are even numbers,
s_{r(\Lambda)}
is an odd number, and
s_{r(\Lambda)}=v_{l}
for some
1\geq l\geq k
.

A pair of integers
(i,j)
is a called a
center
of
\Lambda
, if
(i,j)=(1,v_{k})
when condition (i) holds, or if
(i,j)=(\sum_{t=1}^{l-1}h_{t}+\Lambda_{d_{k}}+1,\sum_{t=l}^{k}v_{t})
when condition (ii) holds. See Figure
2
.

Recall that we order the rows (resp. columns) in
\Xi
from left to right (resp. bottom to top). Therefore, the pair of integers
(i,j)
could be considered the
(i,j)
-box
in
(d\times m)
-frame.

Definition 10.19
.

A
buffalo-check pattern
of size
(d\times m)
is a
d\times m
-frame such that:

(1)

The
(i,j)
-box is colored
black
if both
i
and
j
are odd,

(2)

The
(i,j)
-box is colored
white
if both
i
and
j
are even,

(3)

All other boxes are colored
grey
.

See Figure
3
for an example of a buffalo-check pattern of size
(6\times 6)
.

Definition 10.20
.

A
buffalo-check Young diagram
in
(d\times m)
-frame is a pair
(\Lambda,c_{\Lambda})
consisting of a
K
-even Young diagram
\Lambda
in
(d\times m)
-frame and a center
c_{\Lambda}
of
\Lambda
.

Definition 10.21
.

If
d\equiv l\mod 2
(resp.
d\equiv l-1\mod 2
) for
l\in\mathbb{Z}
, we let
\mathfrak{B}^{l}_{d,m}
be the set of buffalo-check Young diagrams
(\Lambda,(i,j))
for both
i
and
j
odd (resp. either
i
or
j
even).

In particular,
\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}
(resp.
\mathfrak{B}^{d+1}_{d,m}
) is the set of buffalo-check Young diagrams centered at the black boxes (resp. grey boxes) in the buffalo-check pattern. Note that there are no buffalo-check Young diagrams centered at the white boxes in the buffalo-check pattern. Moreover, if we fix an integer
l
, every buffalo-check Young diagram
(\Lambda,c_{\Lambda})\in\mathfrak{B}^{l}_{d,m}
is uniquely determined by the underlying
K
-even Young diagram
\Lambda
. For this reason, we use the
K
-even Young diagram
\Lambda
to indicate the element of
\mathfrak{B}^{l}_{d,m}
instead of the pair
(\Lambda,c_{\Lambda})
.

10.22.
Combinatorial operations on Young diagrams

Let
\mathfrak{C}_{d,m}
be the set of Young diagrams in
(d\times m)
-frame.

Definition 10.23
.

Let
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{d})
be a Young diagram in
\mathfrak{C}_{d,m}
.

(i)

If
\Lambda_{d}\geq 1
, we define the Young diagram
\iota(\Lambda)
in
(d\times(m-1))
-frame as the
d
-tuple
\iota(\Lambda)=(\Lambda_{1}-1,\ldots,\Lambda_{d}-1)
.

(ii)

If
\Lambda_{d}=0
, we define the Young diagram
v(\Lambda)
in
((d-1)\times m)
-frame as the
(d-1)
-tuple
v(\Lambda)=(\Lambda_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{d-1})
.

Remark 10.24
.

Note that
\iota(\Lambda)
(resp.
v(\Lambda)
) deletes the first column (resp. row) of
\Lambda
.

Lemma 10.25
.

Let
\Lambda=(\Lambda,c_{\Lambda})\in\mathfrak{B}^{l}_{d,m}
. Then, we have

(i)

\iota(\Lambda)\in\mathfrak{B}^{l-1}_{d,m-1}
if
\Lambda_{d}\geq 1
;

(ii)

v(\Lambda)\in\mathfrak{B}^{l}_{d-1,m}
if
\Lambda_{d}=0
and
c_{\Lambda}\neq(1,1)
.

Proof.

(i) If
\Lambda_{d}\geq 1
, we note that the sum of the length of all the vertical segments on the left of the center
c_{\Lambda}
is odd. Thus, the buffalo-check Young diagram
\Lambda
can only center at odd rows, where grey and black boxes appear alternately. Now, the operation
\iota(\Lambda)
simultaneously deletes the first column of
\Lambda
and its underlying frame, and observe that the whole buffalo-check pattern therefore moved one column to the left. It follows that the color of the center
c_{\Lambda}
changes from black (resp. grey) to grey (resp. black).

(ii) If
\Lambda_{d}=0
and
c_{\Lambda}\neq(1,1)
, then the buffalo-check Young diagram
\Lambda
can only center at odd columns by a similar reason as in (i). Therefore, the color of the center
c_{\Lambda}
changes from black (resp. grey) to grey (resp. black) under the operation
v
.
∎

Corollary 10.26
.

Let
\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}
. Then, we have

(i)

\iota\iota(\Lambda)\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m-2}
if
\Lambda_{d}\geq 2
;

(ii)

vv(\Lambda)\in\mathfrak{B}^{d-2}_{d-2,m}
if
\Lambda_{d-1}=\Lambda_{d}=0
.

Proof.

If
\Lambda_{d}\geq 2
or
\Lambda_{d-1}=\Lambda_{d}=0
, the buffalo-check Young diagram can not center at
(1,1)
. The result follows by applying Lemma
10.25
twice in each case. ∎

Suppose that
\Pi=(\Pi_{1},\ldots,\Pi_{d})
is an even Young diagram. Let
\rho(\Pi)
be the number of non-zero rows of
\Pi
and let
t(\Pi)
be the half-perimeter of
\Pi
. Recall that
t(\Pi)\equiv\rho(\Pi)+\Pi_{1}\mod 2
(cf.
[
BC12
, Definition 4.3]
).

Definition 10.27
.

For any
l\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define
\mathfrak{A}^{l}_{d,m}
to be the set of even Young diagrams
\Pi
in a
(d\times m)
-frame such that
t(\Pi)\equiv l\mod 2
.

Lemma 10.28
.

Let
\Pi
be an element in
\mathfrak{A}^{l}_{d,m}
. Then, we have

(i)

\iota(\Pi)\in\mathfrak{A}^{l-1}_{d,m-1}
, if
\Pi_{d}\geq 1
;

(ii)

v(\Pi)\in\mathfrak{A}^{l}_{d-1,m}
, if
\Pi_{d}=0
.

Proof.

(i) If
\Pi_{d}\geq 1
, note that the Young diagram
\iota(\Pi)
is even by
[
BC12
, Proposition 2.12 (a)]
. Moreover, we must have
\rho(\Pi)\equiv\rho(\iota(\Pi))\mod 2
. To see this, we observe that
\rho(\Pi)=\rho(\iota(\Pi))
if
\Pi_{d}\geq 2
. If
\Pi_{d}=1
, then
\rho(\Lambda)-\rho(\iota(\Lambda))
is equal to the length the first vertical segment of the boundary of
\Lambda
, which is even. Finally, by definition
\iota(\Pi)_{1}=\Pi_{1}-1
, we see that
t(\iota(\Pi))\equiv l-1\mod 2
.

(ii) If
\Pi_{d}=0
, note that the Young diagram
v(\Pi)
is even by
[
BC12
, Proposition 2.12 (b)]
. By definition
\rho(v(\Pi))=\rho(\Pi)
and
v(\Pi)_{1}=\Pi_{1}
and the result follows.
∎

Corollary 10.29
.

Let
\Pi
be an element in
\mathfrak{A}^{l}_{d,m}
. Then, we have

(i)

\iota\iota(\Pi)\in\mathfrak{A}^{l}_{d,m-2}
, if
\Pi_{d}\geq 2
;

(ii)

vv(\Pi)\in\mathfrak{A}^{l}_{d-2,m}
, if
\Pi_{d-1}=\Pi_{d}=0
.

Proposition 10.30
.

(A)

The following maps of sets

\displaystyle\{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d+1}_{d,m}|\Lambda_{d}\geq 1\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\iota}\mathfrak{B}^{d-1}_{d-1,m}

\displaystyle\{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d+1}_{d,m}|\Lambda_{d}=0\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{v}\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m-1}

\displaystyle\{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d+1}_{d,m}|\Pi_{d}\geq 1\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\iota}\mathfrak{A}^{d-1}_{d-1,m}

\displaystyle\{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d+1}_{d,m}|\Pi_{d}=0\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{v}\mathfrak{A}^{d}_{d,m-1}

are bijective.

(B)

The following maps of sets

\displaystyle\{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}|\Lambda_{d}\geq 2\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\iota\iota}\mathfrak{B}^{d-2}_{d-2,m}

\displaystyle\{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}|\Lambda_{d-1}=\Lambda_{d}=0\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{vv}\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m-2}

\displaystyle\{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d}_{d,m}|\Pi_{d}\geq 2\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\iota\iota}\mathfrak{A}^{d-2}_{d-2,m}

\displaystyle\{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d}_{d,m}|\Pi_{d-1}=\Pi_{d}=0\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{vv}\mathfrak{A}^{d}_{d,m-2}

\{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}|\Lambda_{d}=0,\Lambda_{d-1}\geq 1\}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\iota v}\mathfrak{C}_{d-1,m-1}

are bijective.

Proof.

All the maps of sets are well-defined by Lemma
10.25
,
10.28
and Corollary
10.26
,
10.29
. The proof of the case (A) follows mostly from
[
BC12
, Proposition 2.12]
. The maps
\iota
(resp.
v
) are bijective, since we can construct the inverse maps by adding the a full column (resp. empty row) to the left (resp. bottom) of a Young diagram
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{d})
(resp.
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{d-1})
) to obtain a Young diagram
(\Lambda_{1}+1,\ldots,\Lambda_{d}+1)
(resp.
(\Lambda_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{d-1},0)
) in
(d\times m)
-frame. For the case (B), the bijectivity of all the maps can be obtained from the case (A). For the last bijection, the buffalo-check Young diagram
\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}
that satisfies the conditions
\Lambda_{d}=0
and
\Lambda_{d-1}\geq 1
if and only if it has center
(1,1)
. Furthermore, adding a full column to the left and then an empty row to the bottom of a Young diagram in
((d-1)\times(m-1))
-frame yields a buffalo-check Young diagram
\Lambda
in
(d\times m)
-frame centered at
(1,1)
.
∎

10.31.
Flag variety of a Young diagram

Let
\Lambda
be a Young diagram in the
(d\times m)
-frame
\Xi
. By the previous section, this amounts to
k
-tuples
\underline{d}
and
\underline{m}
. By Section
10.17
and Definition
10.3
, we have an associated flag variety
\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda):=\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{\bullet})=\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k}}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k}})
where
\mathcal{V}_{\bullet}=\{\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}}\lhd\ldots\lhd\mathcal{V}_{r_{k}}\}
with
r_{i}=d_{i}+m_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
. Let

p_{\Lambda}:\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)\to S

be the canonical projection. Recall that
r=d+m
, and we define the following projective morphism

f_{\Lambda}:\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)\xhookrightarrow{\iota}\mathrm{Fl}_{\underline{d}}(\mathcal{V}_{r},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{r})\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}_{r})=\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})

which agrees with the map constructed in
[
BC12
, (15)]
. This yields a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.01392pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.01392pt\raise 39.01392pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Remark 10.32
.

Recall that, by deleting the first row and (resp. column) of
\Lambda
, we obtain the Young diagram
\iota(\Lambda)
(resp.
v(\Lambda)
) in
(d\times(m-1))
-frame (resp.
((d-1)\times m)
-frame). Under our notation, we have canonical projective morphisms

f_{\iota(\Lambda)}:\mathrm{Fl}(\iota(\Lambda))\to\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}_{r-1})=\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})\quad\quad f_{v(\Lambda)}:\mathrm{Fl}(v(\Lambda))\to\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}_{r-1})=\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1})

There is a source of confusion. Note that the targets of the map
f_{\iota(\Lambda)}
(resp.
f_{v(\Lambda)}
) is
\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})
(resp.
\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1})
) rather than
\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})
.

10.33.
Construction of the maps

Let
\omega_{\Lambda}
be the relative canonical bundle of
f_{\Lambda}
.

Definition 10.34
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{Z}
, and let

H_{\Lambda}^{l,n}(L):K(\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda))\rightarrow\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Lambda|]}(\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda),\omega_{\Lambda}\otimes f_{\Lambda}^{*}(L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l}))

be the hyerbolic map. Define the map

\Theta^{l}:\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{l}_{d,m}}K(S)\xrightarrow{\sum\mu_{\Lambda}^{l,n}(L)}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}),L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l}).

where
\mu_{\Lambda}^{l,n}(L)
is defined to be the composition

K(S)\xrightarrow{p_{\Lambda}^{\ast}}K(\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda))\xrightarrow{H_{\Lambda}^{l,n}(L)}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Lambda|]}(\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda),\omega_{\Lambda}\otimes f_{\Lambda}^{*}(L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l}))\xrightarrow{{f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}),L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l})

of maps of spectra.

Lemma 10.35
.

Suppose that
\Pi
is an even Young diagram. Then, the class of the relative canonical bundle
\omega_{\Pi}
in
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Fl}(\Pi))/2
is given by

\omega_{\Pi}=\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\mathcal{V})}\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes t(\Pi)}.

Moreover,
p_{\Pi}^{\ast}(\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})=\omega_{\Pi}\otimes f_{\Pi}^{*}(\Delta_{d}^{\otimes t(\Pi)})\in\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Fl}(\Pi))/2
.

Proof.

See
[
BC12
, Proposition 4.8]
.
∎

Definition 10.36
.

Define the map

\Omega^{l}:\bigoplus_{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{l}_{d,m}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{\sum{\xi_{\Pi}}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}),L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l}).

where the map
\xi_{\Pi}
is the composition

\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{p_{\Pi}^{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(\mathrm{Fl}(\Pi),\omega_{\Pi}\otimes f_{\Pi}^{*}(L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l}))\xrightarrow{{f_{\Pi}}_{\ast}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}),L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l})

of maps of spectra.

Remark 10.37
.

By the projection formula, we see that

\xi_{\Pi}(\alpha):={f_{\Pi}}_{\ast}p_{\Pi}^{\ast}(\alpha)={f_{\Pi}}_{\ast}f_{\Pi}^{\ast}p^{\ast}(\alpha)={f_{\Pi}}_{\ast}(1)\cup p^{\ast}(\alpha)

for any
\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}_{d,m}
and
\alpha\in\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}_{i}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})
. Moreover, by the projection formula on
K
-theory (cf.
[
TT90
, Proposition 3.17]
),

\mu^{l,n}_{\Lambda}(L)(\beta):={f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}H^{l,n}_{\Lambda}(L)p_{\Lambda}^{\ast}(\beta)=H^{l,n}{f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}p_{\Lambda}^{\ast}(\beta)=H^{l,n}({f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}(1)\cup p^{\ast}(\beta))

for any
\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{l}_{d,m}
and
\beta\in K_{i}(S)
, where
H^{l,n}
is the hyperbolic map

(73)

H^{l,n}:K(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}),L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l}).

10.38.
On the main theorem

Now, we are ready to state our main theorem:

Theorem 10.39
.

Let
L
be a line bundle on
S
. Then, the map

(\Theta^{l},\Omega^{l}):\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{l}_{d,m}}K(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{l}_{d,m}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}),L\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes l})

is an equivalence for any
l\in\mathbb{Z}
.

To prove Theorem
10.39
, we need the following propositions in view of notations introduced in Table
2
.

Proposition 10.40
.

Suppose that
\Lambda\in\mathfrak{C}_{d,m}
. Then, for any
\ell=0
or
1
, we have

(1)

\mu^{\ell}_{\Lambda}=\iota_{\ast}\mu^{\ell}_{\iota(\Lambda)}
, if
\Lambda_{d}\geq 1
.

(2)

\mu^{\ell}_{\Lambda}=\pi_{\ast}\alpha^{\ast}\mu^{\ell}_{v(\Lambda)}
, if
\Lambda_{d}=0
.

(3)

\mu^{0}_{\Lambda}=\widehat{\iota}_{*}\mu^{0}_{\iota\iota(\Lambda)}
, if
\Lambda_{d}\geq 2
.

(4)

\mu^{0}_{\Lambda}=(\widecheck{\pi}_{2})_{*}(\widecheck{\pi}_{1})^{*}\mu^{0}_{vv(\Lambda)}
, if
\Lambda_{d-1}=\Lambda_{d}=0
.

Proof.

To prove (1), we note that
\mathrm{Fl}(\iota(\Lambda))=\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)
by definition, and we can form the square

\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}(\iota(\Lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\iota(\Lambda)}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\Lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})}

which is commutative. Observe that

\displaystyle\mu^{\ell}_{\Lambda}:=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}H^{\ell}_{\Lambda}p_{\Lambda}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Definition)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}H^{\ell}_{\iota(\Lambda)}p_{\iota(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Compare the source of $f_{\Lambda*}$ and the target of $p_{\Lambda}^{*}$)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota_{\ast}{f_{\iota(\Lambda)}}_{\ast}H^{\ell}_{\iota(\Lambda)}p_{\iota(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Composition of pushforwards)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota_{\ast}\mu^{\ell}_{\iota(\Lambda)}

\displaystyle\text{(Definition)}.

For the proof of (2), we rewrite
\mathrm{Fl}(v(\Lambda))
into the following two cases:

\mathrm{Fl}(v(\Lambda))=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k-2},d_{k-1}}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k-2}},\mathcal{V}_{r_{k-1}})&\textnormal{ if }d_{k-1}=d-1\\
\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k-1},d-1}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k-1}},\mathcal{V}^{1})&\textnormal{ if }d_{k-1}<d-1\end{cases}

Define the following flag variety

\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(v(\Lambda);\mathcal{V}):=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k}}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k-1}})&\textnormal{ if }d_{k-1}=d-1\\
\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k-1},d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k-1}},\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{V})&\textnormal{ if }d_{k-1}<d-1\end{cases}

Construct the following commutative diagrams

(74)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}(v(\Lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{v(\Lambda)}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(v(\Lambda);\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathring{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}}
\scriptstyle{\acute{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}}
\scriptstyle{\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\Lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{1},\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})}

where the left square is a fibre product, and all the maps are canonical. Note that the morphism
\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}
is projective and birational, and it is even an identity if
d_{k-1}=d-1
. Then by
[
SGA6
, Lemme VII.3.5, p. 441]
, we have that
\mathrm{R}\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{B_{\Lambda}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)}
, and therefore
\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}(1_{B_{\Lambda}})=1_{\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)}
under the pushforward
\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}:K_{0}(\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(v(\Lambda);\mathcal{V}))\to K_{0}(\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda))
. By the projection formula on
K
-theory (cf.
[
TT90
, Proposition 3.17]
), we have
\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}=1
. Now we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mu^{\ell}_{\Lambda}:=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}H^{\ell}_{\Lambda}p_{\Lambda}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Definition)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}H^{\ell}_{\Lambda}\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}p_{\Lambda}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Use $\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}=1$)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}\widehat{H}^{\ell}\acute{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}p_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Pushforwards commute with hyperbolic maps)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}\mathring{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}\widehat{H}^{\ell}\acute{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}p_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Composition of pushforwards)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}\mathring{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}\acute{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}H^{\ell}_{v(\Lambda)}p_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Pullbacks commute with hyperbolic maps)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}\alpha^{\ast}{f_{v(\Lambda)}}_{\ast}H^{\ell}_{v(\Lambda)}p_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\text{(Base change formula)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}\alpha^{\ast}\mu^{\ell}_{v(\Lambda)}.

\displaystyle\text{(Definition)}

where
\widehat{H}^{\ell}
is the hyperbolic map
K(S)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Lambda|]}\big(\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(\iota(\Lambda);\mathcal{V}),\omega_{f_{\Lambda}\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}}\otimes\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}f_{\Lambda}^{\ast}(\mathcal{H}^{\ell})\big)
. Here, the line bundle
\mathcal{H}^{\ell}
is defined to be
\mathcal{H}
if
\ell=0
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}
if
\ell=1
. The result of (2) follows. The proof of (3) is similar to the proof of (1). For the proof of (4), we define the following flag variety

\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(vv(\Lambda);\mathcal{V}):=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k}}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k-1}})&\textnormal{ if }d_{k-1}=d-2\\
\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k-1},d-2,d}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k-1}},\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})&\textnormal{ if }d_{k-1}<d-2\end{cases}

Construct the following commutative diagrams

(75)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}(vv(\Lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{vv(\Lambda)}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(vv(\Lambda);\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathring{\pi}_{vv(\Lambda)}}
\scriptstyle{\acute{\pi}_{vv(\Lambda)}}
\scriptstyle{\grave{\pi}_{vv(\Lambda)}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\Lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d-2}(\mathcal{V}^{2})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Fl}_{d-2,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widecheck{\pi}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\widecheck{\pi}_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})}

where the left square is a fibre product, and all the maps are canonical. Note that the morphism
\grave{\pi}_{vv(\Lambda)}
is projective and birational. The rest of the proof of (4) is then similar to that of (2).
∎

Remark 10.41
.

We do not define
\mu^{1}_{\iota\iota(\Lambda)}
and
\mu^{1}_{vv(\Lambda)}
, as they are not used in this paper. However, Proposition
10.40
(3) and (4) are also true if we define them in the spirit of Table
2
.

Proposition 10.42
.

Suppose that
\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}_{d,m}
. Then, we have

(1)

\xi_{\Pi}=\iota_{\ast}\xi_{\iota(\Pi)}
, if
\Pi_{d}\geq 1
.

(2)

\xi_{\Pi}=\pi_{\ast}\alpha^{\ast}\xi_{v(\Pi)}
, if
\Pi_{d}=0
and
t(\Pi)\equiv d+1\mod 2
.

(3)

\xi_{\Pi}=\widehat{\iota}_{\ast}\xi_{\ \iota(\Pi)}
, if
\Pi_{d}\geq 2
.

(4)

\xi_{\Pi}=(\widecheck{\pi}_{2})_{*}(\widecheck{\pi}_{1})^{*}\xi_{vv(\Pi)}
, if
\Pi_{d-1}=\Pi_{d}=0
and
t(\Pi)\equiv d\mod 2
.

Proof.

The proof of (1) and (3) are similar to that of Proposition
10.40
(1) and (3), as long as we delete the mention of the hyperbolic maps showed up in the proof and replace
\mu
by
\xi
. For the proof of (2), the proof aligned with the proof of Proposition
10.40
(2) in view of the diagram (
74
), except that we only have the identity
\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)\ast}\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}=1
in
\mathrm{GW}
-theory when
\rho(\Pi)\equiv d+1\mod 2
. To explain, we observe that

\omega_{\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}}=(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{V}^{1})^{\otimes d-1-d_{k-1}}\otimes\Delta_{d-1}^{\otimes d-1-d_{k-1}}\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes 1-d+d_{k-1}}

in
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(v(\Lambda);\mathcal{V}))
, and
\omega_{\grave{\pi}_{v(\Lambda)}}=1\in\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(v(\Lambda);\mathcal{V}))/2
when
t(\Pi)=d+1\mod 2
. Here, we use
t(\Pi)\equiv d_{k-1}\mod 2
, cf.
[
BC12
, Proposition 4.5]
. The proof of (4) is similar to the proof of (2) in view of the diagram (
75
). Note that the canonical bundle

\omega_{\grave{\pi}_{vv(\Lambda)}}=\det(\mathcal{V}/\mathcal{V}^{2})^{\otimes d-2-d_{k-1}}\otimes\Delta_{d-2}^{\otimes d-2-d_{k-1}}\otimes\Delta_{d}^{\otimes 2-d+d_{k-1}}

in
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(vv(\Lambda);\mathcal{V}))
, and
\omega_{\grave{\pi}_{vv(\Lambda)}}=1\in\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Fl}_{d}(vv(\Lambda);\mathcal{V}))/2
when
t(\Pi)=d\mod 2
.
∎

Proposition 10.43
.

Let
\Lambda
be a Young diagram in
\mathfrak{C}_{d,m}
such that
\Lambda_{d}=0
and
\Lambda_{d-1}\geq 1
. Then

\mu_{\Lambda}^{0}=\widehat{\pi}_{2\ast}H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{\ast}{f_{\iota v(\Lambda)}}_{\ast}p_{\iota v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}.

Proof.

Note that if
\Lambda_{d}=0
and
\Lambda_{d-1}\geq 1
, then
\Lambda
is a
K
-even Young diagram and the black box
(1,1)
is a center of
\Lambda
. It follows that
(\Lambda,(1,1))\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}
. Recall that
\iota v(\Lambda)
is a Young diagram in
((d-1)\times(m-1))
-frame, cf. Proposition
10.30
(B). To proceed, we rewrite the flag variety

\mathrm{Fl}(\iota v(\Lambda))=\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k-1}}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k-1}})

If
\Lambda_{d}=0
and
\Lambda_{d-1}\geq 1
, then we must have

d_{k}=d,d_{k-1}=d-1,r_{k}=d+m\textnormal{ and }r_{k-1}\leq d+m-2.

Next, we form the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}(\iota v(\Lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\iota v(\Lambda)}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm\framebox{$\scriptstyle 76$}}{}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\pi}_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\bar{f}_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\Lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(\mathcal{V})}

where the left diagram is a fiber square and the map
\bar{f}_{\Lambda}
is defined to be the following composition

\mathrm{Fl}(\Lambda)=\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k}}(\mathcal{V}_{r_{1}},\ldots,\mathcal{V}_{r_{k}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathrm{Fl}_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{k-2},d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\ldots,\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V})\xrightarrow{\pi}\mathrm{Fl}_{d-1,d}(\mathcal{V}^{2},\mathcal{V}).

Then by the base change formula on
K
-theory (cf.
[
TT90
, Proposition 3.18]
) and Lemma
3.15
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mu^{0}_{\Lambda}:=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}H^{0}_{\Lambda}p_{\Lambda}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Definition)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{d,n}{f_{\Lambda}}_{\ast}p_{\Lambda}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Pushforwards commute with hyperbolic maps)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{d,n}\widehat{\pi}_{2\ast}\bar{f}_{\Lambda\ast}\bar{\pi}_{\Lambda}^{\ast}p_{\iota v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Compositions of pushforwards and pullbacks)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{2\ast}H\bar{f}_{\Lambda\ast}\bar{\pi}_{\Lambda}^{\ast}p_{\iota v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Pushforwards commute with hyperbolic maps)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{2\ast}H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{\ast}{f_{\iota v(\Lambda)}}_{\ast}p_{\iota v(\Lambda)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\textnormal{(Base change formula)}

where
H^{d,n}
is the hyperbolic map defined in (
73
) and
H
is the hyperbolic map defined in (
71
).
∎

10.44.
Proof of Theorem
10.39

If
d=1
, the statement of Theorem
10.39
is true by Theorem
9.6
. If
d\geq 1
, the results follow by the induction setp below. Assume that the theorem holds for any
\mathrm{Gr}_{d^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime})
with either
d^{\prime}<d
and
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime})\leq\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{V})
or
d^{\prime}\leq d
and
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{V}^{\prime})<\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{V})
.

Case (A)
If
l\equiv d+1\mod 2
, then we need to show that the map

(\Theta^{d+1},\Omega^{d+1}):\bigoplus_{{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d+1}_{d,m}}}K(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d+1}_{d,m}}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))

is an equivalence in view of Table
1
. By Theorem
10.12
(A), we have an equivalence

\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\big(\iota_{\ast},\pi_{\ast}\tilde{\alpha}^{\ast}\big)}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d}(\mathcal{V})).

By the induction hypothesis and Proposition
10.30
(A), the maps

\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d+1}_{d,m}}^{\Lambda_{d}\geq 1}K(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d+1}_{d,m}}^{\Pi_{d}\geq 1}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{\big(\sum\mu^{1}_{\iota(\Lambda)},\sum\xi_{\iota(\Pi)}\big)}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-d]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))

\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d+1}_{d,m}}^{\Lambda_{d}=0}K(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d+1}_{d,m}}^{\Pi_{d}=0}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{\big(\sum\mu^{1}_{v(\Lambda)},\sum\xi_{v(\Pi)}\big)}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{1}))

are equivalences. The result follows from Proposition
10.40
(1) and (2), and
10.42
(1) and (2).

Case (B)
Suppose that
l\equiv d\mod 2
. we need to show that the map

(\Theta^{d},\Omega^{d}):\bigoplus_{{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}}}K(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d}_{d,m}}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))

is an equivalence in view of Table
1
. By Lemma
10.16
, the map

K(\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n-2d]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))\oplus\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d-2}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))\xrightarrow{((\widehat{\pi}_{2})_{*}H(\widehat{\pi}_{1})^{*},\widehat{\iota}_{*},(\widecheck{\pi}_{2})_{*}(\widecheck{\pi}_{1})^{*})}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}))

is an equivalence.
By the induction hypothesis and Proposition
10.30
(B), the maps

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}}^{\Lambda_{d}\geq 2}K(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d}_{d,m}}^{\Pi_{d}\geq 2}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{\big(\sum\mu^{0}_{\iota\iota(\Lambda)},\sum\xi_{\iota(\iota(\Pi))}\big)}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-2d]}(\mathbb{G}_{d}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}}}^{\Lambda_{d-1}=\Lambda_{d}=0}K(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}^{d}_{d,m}}}^{\mathclap{\Pi_{d-1}=\Pi_{d}=0}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}(S,L\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\otimes\rho(\Pi)})\xrightarrow{\big(\sum\mu^{0}_{vv(\Lambda)},\sum\xi_{v(v(\Pi))}\big)}\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathbb{G}_{d-2}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathfrak{B}^{d}_{d,m}}^{\Lambda_{d-1}\geq 1,\Lambda_{d}=0}K(S)\xrightarrow{(\sum{f_{\iota v(\Lambda)}}_{\ast}p_{\iota v(\Lambda)}^{\ast})}K(\mathrm{Gr}_{d-1}(\mathcal{V}^{2}))

are equivalences. The result follows from Proposition
10.40
(3)(4),
10.42
(3)(4) and
10.43
.

Theorem 10.45
.

The map

(\Theta^{0}+\Theta^{1},\Omega^{0}+\Omega^{1}):\bigoplus_{(\Lambda,c_{\Lambda})\in\mathfrak{B}_{d,m}}K_{i}(S)\oplus\bigoplus_{\Pi\in\mathfrak{A}_{d,m}}\mathrm{GW}^{[n-|\Pi|]}_{i}(S,L)\to\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}_{i}(\mathrm{Gr}_{d}(m),L)^{\mathrm{tot}}

is an equivalence of spectra.

Proof.

Note that the trivial bundle
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{O}^{\oplus d+m}_{S}
admits a canonical complete flag. Moreover, we have the following disjoint union of sets

\mathfrak{A}_{d,m}=\mathfrak{A}_{d,m}^{d}\bigsqcup\mathfrak{A}_{d,m}^{d+1}\text{\quad and \quad}\mathfrak{B}_{d,m}=\mathfrak{B}_{d,m}^{d}\bigsqcup\mathfrak{B}_{d,m}^{d+1}.

The result follows immediately from Theorem
10.39
.
∎

Example 10.46
.

See Figure
4
and
5
for examples of the indexing set for the additive basis of Hermitian
K
-theory of
\mathrm{Gr}_{2}(2)
and
\mathrm{Gr}_{3}(3)
.

Figure 4.
The indexing set for the additive basis of
\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Gr}_{2}(2))^{\mathrm{tot}}
. The first line consists of even Young diagrams in
(2\times 2)
-frame with thickened inner-frame boundary segments; The second line (resp. third line) includes buffalo-check Young diagrams in
\mathfrak{B}^{2}_{2,2}
(resp.
\mathfrak{B}^{3}_{2,2}
) with center hatched.

Figure 5.
The indexing set for the additive basis of
\mathrm{GW}^{[n]}(\mathrm{Gr}_{3}(3))^{\mathrm{tot}}
. The first line consists of even Young diagrams in
(3\times 3)
-frame with thickened inner-frame boundary segments; The second line (resp. third line) includes buffalo-check Young diagrams in
\mathfrak{B}^{3}_{3,3}
(resp.
\mathfrak{B}^{4}_{3,3}
) with center hatched.
